Time was running out.

"Thank you. For everything."

"Mm...."

We rose from our seats.

I handed him a box of donuts I had separately asked the counter to pack to-go, along with an extra empty paper bag holding only a bread knife.

"By the way, what kind of wish did you have when you first joined this company, Mr. J?"

"......."

The Guard Captain looked into the empty bag and said:

"Honestly... I don't really remember."

"......."

"Don’t worry about it... it wasn’t anything important."

I couldn’t ask any further.

In the end, I nodded.

In front of the donut shop, we said our farewells.

The Guard Captain waved at me.

"...Get home safe."

And that was the end.

Through a shadow cast on the ground, I could tell that the Guard Captain stood there, motionless, for quite a long time after I left.

"......."

On my way back, I thought to myself.

...I should say goodbye to the others too.

Chapter 207

"Ah—right! Please send that to the team lead’s desk, Agent Bakha."

"Yes...!"

Ko Yeongeun carried the freshly organized stack of documents over to her superior’s desk.

They contained information on a newly observed supernatural disaster.

Feels strange that I'm more used to the term ‘disaster’ now than I am to ‘darkness’...

Hoo.

Ko Yeongeun sat back down at her desk.

She could faintly hear agents sharing small talk out in the hallway.

"...That’s how it happened. Hyeonmu Team 1...."

"But honestly, if I were that rookie, I think I would’ve done the same thing...."

"......."

Her nerves immediately shot up.

Even just that much was enough to know who they were talking about.

It had been two weeks since field agents at the Disaster Response Agency began speaking cautiously—but frequently—about one particular person.

Agent Podo.

Specifically, her spy colleague.

Kim Soleum’s incarceration, release... and disappearance.

Yes.

Ko Yeongeun’s colleague had failed in his spy mission and vanished.

...Though maybe it was lucky he hadn’t been captured.

Judging from the situation, something had happened when Director Ho’s Taboo Seal was exposed. But given the hush-hush atmosphere, it was hard to ask directly.

She knew it had gotten pretty serious during his imprisonment, then seemed to have stabilized again.

And even after his disappearance, the field agents didn’t seem particularly hostile toward Kim Soleum.

...News from Hyeonmu Team 1 had dropped off dramatically, too.

That was about all Ko Yeongeun could confirm from her position as a rookie agent.

Of course, once Kim Soleum had vanished without a trace right after being released from the Glass Prison, there was no realistic way to find out more.

He really... did escape, right?

Her mind tensed with worry again, but she could only end the train of thought with a sigh.

If she recklessly tried to dig into his whereabouts and ended up leaking information, it could be bad.

If Kim Soleum had successfully escaped, interfering now would only be a nuisance. Worse, it might expose her own identity.

It was a lose-lose situation.

Given her somewhat familiar relationship with Kim Soleum—and the fact that they were both rookies—if someone were monitoring her for safety reasons, it would only make things worse.

...I think they suspect me of being a spy too.

She didn’t know for sure.

The Disaster Response Agency people were clearly kind, but they also had a way of casually digging at you without showing it—and sometimes hitting the mark dead-on.

Well, I mean. They are agents, after all.

Still, despite her tightrope-walking nerves, the agents hadn’t changed their attitude toward her.

She hadn’t been dragged off to the Glass Prison or anything like that.

That’s a relief, but...

...Honestly, it felt hopeless.

Of the three rookies who’d joined, one had been quarantined due to contamination, one had disappeared.

She was the only one left—and for a single rookie to extract an extinction-level disaster document on her own? That was practically impossible.

...If she were Director Ho, she would’ve cut her losses by now.

But I have to endure.

And get the job done.

Ko Yeongeun steadied herself, keeping panic at bay by calmly calculating her next steps.

Stay cool. One thing at a time.

First, she needed to clean up any remaining traces.

She’d only ever contacted Kim Soleum through a separate phone, registered under a different name, one she had set up herself.

It was time to deal with that, too.

That evening, after getting off work, she returned to her officetel and immediately went looking for the burner phone.

Rather than hide it in an overly elaborate way, she’d carelessly tossed it in a drawer among other worn-out junk—just like an old phone.

That way, it was easier to overlook.

And the moment she powered it on—

Spam texts poured in, just as planned.

Even the messages from Director Ho disguised as “counseling invitations” looked indistinguishable from junk mail.

"Hoo..."

She began methodically deleting them one by one.

[Stop sitting there calmly—get off now! At this stop ★Special Discount★ Gangnam Station...]

Her hand froze.

"...Wait a second."

“Calmly,” “get off,” “this stop”...

“...!”

Like a lightning bolt, it hit her.

Shin-Yeon Transit Corporation’s subway.

This was a coded message only someone who’d survived that insane recruitment urban legend would understand.

And there was only one person who would send such a thing at this point in time, disguised as spam—

Only one.

Ko Yeongeun immediately opened the message.

[Stop sitting there calmly—get off now! At this stop, only until 21:00 on the 17th—★Special Discount★ Gangnam Station Discount Phone Shop Left Counter (Free Unsubscribe)]

"......."

A place. A time.

Ko Yeongeun quickly searched online.

A discount phone shop near Gangnam Station.

An hour later.

Ko Yeongeun stood near a coin locker next to the phone shop.

"......."

At first, she wondered if they were just going to meet in front of the store.

But even after the stated time—9 PM—no one showed up.

Then, she remembered the “left counter” mentioned in the message. She looked to the left side of the shop—

and suddenly, a hallway leading to the coin lockers revealed itself, as if it had been hiding in plain sight.

Is this it?

She hesitated briefly, then scanned the locker numbers.

There it was: locker 17.

Just like the number specified in the message.

"......."

In the password field, she entered the numeric combination from the message: 1721.

Beep-beep-beep-beep—click.

"......!"

The locker unlocked.

Ko Yeongeun hastily grabbed the payment receipt card and peeked inside the compartment.

Inside was a small paper bag.

Attached was a jagged, awkwardly written note, clearly scribbled with an unfamiliar hand:

Open at home.

"......."

Ko Yeongeun clutched the envelope and rushed back home.

Her heart pounded.

What is this?

All signs pointed to one person—it had to be Kim Soleum.

But what on earth was he trying to give her?

Had the spy mission protocol changed to involve this kind of drop-off communication? Her mind swirled with theories as she sat at her dining table and opened the bag.

Two boxes?

There were black boxes inside.

Perplexed, Ko Yeongeun opened one.

A brilliant rainbow-colored light spilled out.

"...!!"

As if electrocuted, the Baekilmong employee snapped the lid shut again.

A—A Wish Ticket!

That insane holographic aurora-like glow—

there was no mistaking it. It was a Wish Ticket!

Kim Soleum-ssi!

Did he actually complete the spy mission by himself and secure the Wish Ticket?!

How? Was this real? Was any of this even real? There was a Wish Ticket in a subway locker—hold on.

...There were two boxes.

She could hear the sound of her heartbeat ringing in her skull.

She reached for the other box and opened it—

Again, that dazzling, shimmering light spilled out.

Oh my god.

And between the two glowing boxes, a small note slipped out.

“...!”

She quickly picked it up.

Yeongeun-ssi.

Our work is done.

Do what you want.

There’s no need to return.

"......."

A shock so heavy it left her dazed.

But the note wasn’t over yet.

P.S.) If possible, please deliver the other box to its intended recipient.

If not, I ask you to keep it safe until then.

"What the...!"

What if I just take it for myself?—was he seriously handing over two full bottles of Wish Ticket with just a note like this?!

...But even as she thought that, this kind, clever spy knew—

That she would keep her word.

"......."

A whirlwind of gratitude, guilt, joy, and pressure stirred through her head.

So—

Ko Yeongeun made one more quiet decision.

Then she grabbed something and ran out of the house.

Delivered safely.

I saw that the drink I’d been sipping at the café had reached the bottom.

This was a coffee shop in Gangnam Station’s underground mall.

It had already been an hour and a half since I’d watched Ko Yeongeun pick up the phone from the subway locker.

Nothing out of the ordinary had happened since, so I figured it was time to head out.

That takes care of that.

...If it was Ko Yeongeun, I was confident she wouldn’t steal the Wish Ticket and would get it to the rightful owner.

And since she was still working at the Disaster Response Agency, she’d probably have more chances to access people than I would.

...It did seem like the job suited her better, too.

Now that the spy work was over, if she wanted to stay on at the agency, she probably could.

Luckily, there hadn’t been any tailing agents either.

...It all wrapped up pretty cleanly.

Now, only one Wish Ticket potion remained in my hands.

So if I just drink it now...

That’s what I was thinking when—

A familiar figure came running back toward the locker.

"...!"

...It was Ko Yeongeun.

Did something go wrong?

But she calmly reopened locker 17—the one where the Wish Tickets had been—and put something inside, then shut it tight.

After that, she walked away like nothing had happened.

"......."

What did she put in the locker?

Wait... did she return Jang Heoun’s Wish Ticket?

That could be. Honestly, I wouldn’t blame her—

asking someone to pass a Wish Ticket to a quarantined person they might never see again was asking a lot.

Yeah.

It was an unreasonable favor to begin with.

I waited long enough to confirm Ko Yeongeun was fully gone and that no one was tailing her. Then I quietly left the café and headed to the locker.

I opened locker 17.

...Sure enough, there was a box inside.

Can’t be helped.

I picked it up.

But... something felt off about the weight.

"......?"

I quickly slipped into a restroom stall and carefully opened the box.

Instead of a shimmering rainbow, a soft blue light emerged.

A pale green hue, like a newborn sprout.

A Class C Regeneration Potion.

"......."

I stared blankly at it.

There was a note inside the box.

Written just like I’d done.

I’ll do my best.

Thank you.

Take care of yourself.

An emotion I couldn’t describe stormed inside me.

She probably only had enough points to buy one bottle.

She must have thought about the fact that I didn’t have an arm anymore...

and that I couldn’t use foxfire prosthetics after leaving the Disaster Response Agency.

...Thank you.

I carefully packed the box and tucked it close to my chest.

That night.

I drank the regeneration potion.

And with both arms fully restored, I sent my goodbyes.

Scheduled messages, set to be delivered the next morning.

To my former D-Team supervisors... and to a few people I owed a debt to.

Let’s send one more to the Guard Captain too.

Most of the messages said the same thing—

that I was leaving safely, so they didn’t have to worry.

Writing the message to Section Chief Lee Jahaeon gave me cold sweats, though.

The alien shop installment plan...

I’m truly sorry. I ended up skipping town just after the first payment.

In return, I’d put every piece of equipment I owned into a subway locker, then sent Jahaeon the location and password.

I could only hope he’d forgive me with that.

Hoo.

And with that, everything was sorted.

All that remained... was the foxfire.

I lifted my newly-regenerated arm and showed it to the small blue flame that had been floating near me, watching what I did with curiosity.

"It’s okay now. Look, I’ve got both arms again. You can go back."

But the foxfire flickered sharply and sat itself down in front of my desk, as if refusing.

It was inconvenient... but not unpleasant.

"Then stay for a little while longer. ...Once I disappear, go back to the agency. Got it?"

The foxfire bobbed gently, like a small nod, its glow slightly dimmed.

Then now, truly—only one thing remained.

The last.

I pulled out the soft, fuzzy, pink rabbit plushie.

"......."

I took a deep breath and placed the “Nice Friend” doll at the foot of the bed, casting its shadow under the lamplight.

So it could speak, even in reality.

—Oh, friend!

That familiar host’s voice, cheerful as ever.

That slick, drawn-out tone that had become so familiar by now addressed me smoothly.

—This hotel isn’t bad at all tonight. A small, cozy space—makes me think of the room we shared with Miss Roe. Is this a night of wish-fulfillment celebration?

"Something like that."

I didn’t need to save money anymore.

And...

"......."

—Friend?

"Are you okay?"

—What do you mean?

"I mean... about me using the Wish Ticket."

It had been weighing on me for a while. But I was afraid of what kind of answer I’d get, so I hadn’t asked.

"You know what my wish is."

The host already knew.

If he’d peeked into my thoughts and knowledge even a little—no, at the very least, after listening to my conversation with the Guard Captain, he’d have figured it out.

That I was going to wish to leave this world.

"And yet you never once asked."

I kept talking, watching the silhouette of the giant plushie.

"It was weird."

—Oh...

"I wondered if maybe... you thought I’d fail, and were just quietly waiting for the catharsis."

—My word.

As I’ve said before, friend, I do not wish for your failure...! I merely refrain from interfering inappropriately and ruining the flow and staging of the scene!

And in this case, well...

A faint tapping sound, like gloves patting gently.

—It’s such a perfectly clear situation that no question is needed.

You want to go home, do you not? And yet you refused even Brown’s offer to introduce you to a newer, better home....

A chill ran down my spine.

But the host’s tone was still pleasant and serene.

—So as the host, I believe it’s only right to sit back and see where your choice leads this show.

"......."

—Do as you please, friend.

You’ve worked for this, after all. Now it’s time to see what comes of it.

The rabbit doll’s shadow swayed slightly, as if gesturing toward something with its hand.

The Wish Ticket potion on the table.

...Hoo.

—Let’s go, then. To the finale, and the ending!

I picked up the Wish Ticket.

Chapter 208 pt 1

The iridescent potion ripples in my hands.

The color of a dream.

The potion that grants a wish.

...Its label, engraved in delicate relief on the transparent glass bottle, reveals itself whenever the potion inside shimmers like waves of light.

I stared into it.

: Baekilmong Potion :

Wish

EX (miraculous)

Hold the bottle with both hands.

After expressing your earnest wish in a single sentence...

Close your eyes and drink the entire contents at once.

Any wish will be granted.

I’d known the instructions for a long time.

Which is why I’d been agonizing over it from the beginning.

What sentence should I use?

When using the Wish Ticket, in what single sentence could I express my wish?

Every time I lay in bed unable to sleep, every time I wrote up an exploration report in the office, every time I staggered home after barely surviving an urban legend—I thought about it.

The very first phrase that came to mind was:

A primal urge.

“Please send me home.”

It was desperate.

But I decided against it.

Home was too vague a concept.

I’d lived in too many places for the potion to reliably interpret what I meant.

There was no way to know what it would use as its reference point.

Worse, it might even send me back to the Baekilmong dorms.

...Even during my time undercover at the Disaster Response Agency, I’d gone out of my way to stay in motels instead of anything that might feel like “home.” That discomfort probably stemmed from this very thought.

Anyway, I kept thinking.

I had to eliminate all ambiguity.

I can’t mess this up.

I had to ensure the Wish Ticket didn’t interpret my wish in some unintended way.

“Send me back to where I used to live”? Still too vague. Rejected.

“Send me to a world without urban legends”? No guarantee that’d be the one I came from. For all I knew, it could mean I’d die and go to heaven, which technically is a world without urban legends...

I discarded possibility after possibility, meticulously narrowing it down.

A sentence that could specify time and space with absolute precision.

And I decided.

Clutching the potion tightly in both hands, I opened my mouth and spoke.

"My wish is—"

Here it is.

“To return to the place I was the day before I joined Baekilmong Corporation.”

The world I originally lived in.

The moment before I ever spun that roulette at the popup store.

A perfect designation.

“Splendid!”

It worked.

I heard the host's admiring exclamation and calmly opened the potion bottle.

Light burst out, but I covered it with my hand and closed my eyes.

Then, I poured the potion into my mouth.

"......!"

A dream you drink.

That’s the only way to describe it.

A bizarre liquid, synesthetic and unlike anything I’d ever tasted, slid down my throat.

It filled my stomach with radiant brilliance.

Formless power. Transcendent authority. Overflowing mystery.

Ecstasy and awe surged through me.

Yes. I’m in transit!

Oh my god.

My whole body is being pulled somewhere.

Deep—far—

To the place where my wish leads.

It feels like I’m embarking on a pilgrimage to fulfill a lifelong desire.

A supernatural force is relocating me.

Yes.

Yes!

I’ll finally return safely to my—

......

......?

“Guh—!”

I collapsed to the floor.

My body tumbled onto a dusty, filthy surface as a cough tore from my throat.

A musty, sour air.

When I opened my eyes, there was nothing but pitch-black darkness. Even blinking revealed no outline.

Silence.

Silence.

What... is this?

The fantastical sensation was gone. The Wish Ticket had vanished. Which meant—

This was my destination.

"......."

The blood drained from my head.

I felt numb.

“Ugh.”

I twisted myself upright, my arms and hands scraping against sharp fragments that left burning stings behind.

Panting, I looked at the empty bottle I still held in one hand.

The last few drops of potion shimmered faintly like dying embers.

The space around me came into view.

...I was lying on shattered glass.

"...!"

I turned my head and saw where I’d fallen.

A broken, empty glass chamber.

I had collapsed onto shards of shattered glass within a dust-filled pit.

...Why?

Why was I... transported into that?

My heart began to thud in dread.

Before the fear could take over, I desperately searched the shadows for a silhouette of the device.

It looked like a large container once used to hold liquid. And yet—

...The device looked familiar.

No power, no lights, and beneath the broken glass tank was a panel of buttons and a keyboard—

an old-fashioned experimental machine, early 2000s style.

That was—that was...

The Dream Incubator.

A broken, ruined Dream Incubator from Yukhoe Research Lab.

......

I blankly scanned my surroundings.

My eyes, now adjusted to the darkness and guided by the potion’s leftover glow, finally recognized the space.

This was—

The prototype Dream Incubation Room of Yukhoe Research Lab.

"......!"

Something had gone wrong.

Something had gone very wrong.

Why was I here?

"No..."

Stay calm. Stay calm. I clearly asked to return to where I was the day before joining—why am I here—wait. No. No.

"This isn’t right!"

It’s different!

Yeah. The layout was the same as that old lab—but something felt off.

The silhouettes of the machines and documents looked different.

There were cassette tapes and empty reagent bottles. And computers...

The computers don’t have dust on them.

......That’s strange.

I staggered and turned my head. I needed a clue—anything.

And then I saw it.

A research journal.

"......."

Soaked in blood and filth, all the records inside had been erased, replaced by a single phrase written over and over:

We were deceived.

It sat beside the broken Dream Incubator.

Just like the one I had found last time in the Dream Incubation Room.

"......."

I approached it.

This one wasn’t soaked in blood or filth, but it had been scorched brown.

I turned the pages with trembling hands.

Burnt in parts, the pages were difficult to read—but not impossible.

Right from the first page:

Research Invitation: To the Name

I drew in a breath.

Research Goal:

To confirm the target...

To induce the descent of the ...being into this world, and proceed with the contract.

We have adopted the name used by a recently popular cult that worships this being, derived from prior foundational research.

This will be the final project of Yukhoe Research Lab.

My heart began pounding ominously.

I flipped through the pages in a frenzy.

Endless records of experiments.

Each entry varied in format and executor—since they were never registered as official data.

All sorts of trials and preparations.

Failure. Dream Incubator #21 discarded.

Failure after failure.

Horrific ingredients and extreme methods, half of them redacted or smeared out.

They’d created something. Summoned something. Then discarded it.

And then—

Success.

A meaningful result had emerged.

Experiment #316

Solution volume: 1200L

Ingredients: Qterw-A-4’s ...drake, ......-...326, human blood, ......

Thirteen other synthetic elements.

Succeeded in awakening the mind of the Name within the glass chamber.

Contract failed. Form collapsed within solution.

Suspected cause: insufficient fluid volume.

Future trials will adjust variables based on this result.

And the records grew more refined from there.

Experiment #322

Solution volume: 2300L

The awakened Name let out a shriek from within the glass chamber... then showed no further signs of life.

After recognizing the mutation of its own body, a rejection response occurred, breaking down the combined materials into a formless mass.

Request to adjust ingredient ratios in order to prepare a vessel that more accurately reflects the Name’s true appearance.

Currently investigating darkness-related to shapeshifting.

My hands trembled.

A dreadful suspicion began to loop in my mind.

The experiments continued, and their conditions grew increasingly specific.

The current known requirements for summoning and contracting with the Name are as follows:

Preservation of the Name’s original identity

Erasure of previous emotional attachments

Provision of a survival objective

Elimination of all cognitive dissonance

It was believed that powerful Taboo Seals could help fulfill these conditions, and multiple methods were being proposed.

And then—

An old invention by Yukhoe Research Lab was mentioned.

A now-discontinued product designed to implant a piece of an entity from the Other Side into a doll, so it could serve the contractor in a friendly, supportive role.

A limited version of the product was currently being distributed as a souvenir in a certain board game—

That was it.

The product known as “Nice Friend.”

"......."

I managed to turn the page.

The final experiment record.

Experiment #444

Solution volume: 39,999L

Ingredients: Same as #443

Result: Success.

Utilizing the ritual from the discontinued product, the Name was fully summoned.

Stabilized... within [REDACTED].

Upon awakening, it is to be removed from the dream-solution immediately and subjected to brief questioning.

The page ended there.

But—

"......."

When I flipped past the final sheet, I saw a crumpled scrap of torn paper tucked behind it.

Wrinkled, as if it had once been balled up and tossed—then carefully smoothed out and placed back with the experiment records by someone else.

On it, hastily scribbled writing:

Baekilmong found out. They stormed in. Another trick. Before they could steal the Name, I smuggled it out and hid it... in the place with the most people.

Please—

The sentence cut off abruptly.

Below the line “the place with the most people,” someone had underlined it heavily and scrawled a different note in a separate handwriting.

New Employee Orientation?

As if the writer had deduced it from context.

"......."

No.

My mind rejected it.

But the realization had already happened.

There was no need to guess.

It was obvious.

That this—

This was the answer to my wish:

“Please return me to the place I was the day before I joined Baekilmong Corporation.”

And in response—

I had awakened inside this broken Dream Incubator.

Which meant—

I was...

The subject of this research journal.

Nice Friend.

The entity from the Other Side, summoned by Yukhoe Research Lab and fused with a synthetic being.

Projected into a plush toy—like the host, Brown.

The consciousness of Kim Soleum, from another world, projected into something created in the Dream Incubator.

...Was that it?

"No..."

No.

There must be another explanation.

What did the Taboo Seal say again? I don’t qualify. I want to go home. I must go home.

I have to go back to my house, to my family, my friends—

So then...

Who exactly are those people?

"......."

I can’t remember.

I mean—

I’m sure I remembered them back when I first joined, right?

Didn’t I recall conversations with my parents and friends? Didn’t I go over my contacts, check records, verify locations—

...Did I?

Or did I just assume I had done that?

Can’t remember a single number I looked up.

Did I ever actually visit an old address?

I don’t know.

I don’t remember.

Just the belief that I had.

"......."

So am I—

This body I’m in—

Not even Kim Soleum?

If this was the place I’d been the day before joining,

If I returned to the place this body had been,

If I was created in a dream research lab—

Am I... not even human?

"No."

I looked down at my hands.

[No]

No!

Tha can’t be

My body bulged and distorted.

The tattoos shattered.

I was disappearing. The human form collapsed.

A self aware of the truth rejected the human body. It began reverting to its original state.

A sacred, grotesque, incomprehensible fusion emerged from inside me.

Scales, horns, hooves, barbs—ripping through ribs—

"No!"

My voice reverberated strangely in the air.

But there was nothing I could do.

I had already disposed of every item.

My tattoos were empty—

Wa i t—

In that moment.

I realized something.

There was still one thing left in my tattoo.

Something I’d borrowed—

Not mine.

"...!"

With a mutating hand, I clawed into the tattoo and yanked out the equipment.

The Security Team suit.

I began frantically forcing it on.

My deforming body was shoved, compressed into the humanoid frame.

Dark smoke hissed around me as the strange form was sealed inside.

I barely managed to regain a human shape.

I had to run.

Staggering, I searched for the lab door. My glowing yellow eyes caught the doorknob in the dark.

I reached for it and pushed myself out—

—

A fluorescent-lit office hallway.

"......!"

A scene I’d seen before.

...Baekilmong’s underground.

Just as the Guard Captain had told me.

The Yukhoe Research Lab... still beneath company HQ.

“The lab’s still down there...”

“Go deeper underground... and the same office hallway repeats endlessly...”

What floor was this?

How do I get out?

Elevator...

My head and body were heavy.

An intense, crushing pain coursed through every limb.

Dragging my feet, I stumbled toward the end of the hallway—

And then.

"Impressive."

.......

"Such devotion. Coming back like this."

I raised my head.

Someone in a business suit stood before me, staring me down.

An age-defying presence, with a clean-cut appearance, navy-black hair, and a °• N o v e l i g h t •° flawless three-button suit.

Executive Director Cheongdallae.

"Already dressed in uniform. How admirable."

His dark pupils bore into me.

"But that uniform’s not quite right..."

I opened my mouth—

But no words came out.

My vocal cords felt compressed inside the suit.

The awful, strangling discomfort grew stronger—more painful.

"Ah. So that thing lets a human appear monstrous. Is it a new model?"

"......."

"But when a real monster wears it, it’s just a mold forcing the body inside.

As you know, in this world, most programs don’t work unless a contract is signed."

And then, the declaration:

"In that state, you’ll start to rot."

"......."

"If you don’t like that, there is another way."

Cheongdallae pulled a crisp sheet of paper from his jacket.

"A contract."

.......

"Just sign. I promise humane treatment.

You’ll have a fulfilling role...

On the Security Team."

Blood began dripping from my fingers.

"Here."

"......."

"Make your choice."

I was going to die.

My ruined body twisted, my mind unraveling.

I couldn’t even walk anymore.

No strength to consider another path.

A dead end.

"......."

I raised a trembling hand.

And signed the contract with my blood.

"Good."

As the signature finished,

My body was instantly stabilized.

Like slipping into a perfectly tailored suit.

Like fulfilling a promise.

Like pouring the exact amount of water into a glass.

Calm spread through me—

Along with a strange new power.

And, most of all, a distinct Taboo Seal.

"......."

I looked at my superior.

"You’ve just been assigned a truly exceptional role."

And that’s how...

I started working for the company again.

* The END OF PART 1 *

Chapter 209

6 PM, as the sun began to set.

A dusky glow spilled across the entrance of a building in Seoul.

A cold, pristine ground floor space, designed in a modern style.

(Ltd.) Baekilmong Corp. – Annex Building

Entry restricted to authorized personnel only.

It was the lobby of Baekilmong Corporation’s annex building.

But this stark space, which should have been desolate and somber, now buzzed with subdued energy from the murmuring of people.

“Wow, the atmosphere...”

“Hello! Yes, yes. Nice to meet you...”

Several dozen young professionals, clad in black suits, were mingling, exchanging greetings.

Their faces were bright, filled with anticipation and nervous energy.

It was no wonder.

“Congratulations on joining!”

“Hahaha, thank you! You too!”

They had just been accepted into Baekilmong, a high-profile pharmaceutical company that had become a hot topic on the stock market in recent years.

Accordingly, the company had sent out emails summoning them to the annex building next to its Seoul headquarters.

New Employee Orientation Notice

Today was the very first day of orientation.

“A 3-day, 2-night OT at the Seoul annex? That’s kind of luxurious, isn’t it?”

“For real. Wow, this looks like the lobby of an art museum.”

“Pfft, this is what you’d expect from a major corporation... Ah, is everyone here from sales?”

“Seems like it. From what I’ve heard, everyone’s in sales.”

“Then maybe the other departments’ new hires are gathered somewhere else nearby.”

Drunk on the joy of having passed, the new employees chatted with bright smiles.

There was no reason not to be cheerful—after all, they were the victors who had landed jobs at this pharmaceutical giant, and they’d be seeing these fellow new hires regularly from now on.

As a result, a few of them even took the initiative to strike up conversations with those left out of the group chatter.

“Hey there, you're a new hire, right?”

“...”

Amid the noisy group of new employees, someone who had been quietly staring into the annex interior lifted his head.

A young man around the same age, with a sallow, hollow complexion.

Sensing a chance to lead the conversation, the friendly new hire continued speaking with a slight air of self-importance.

“Congrats on joining! Let’s do our best together!”

But...

“Thank you.”

That was it.

“...”

“...”

Silence.

What the hell.

What kind of new sales hire is that reserved?

How the hell did he pass the interview?

Sensing the atmosphere turning awkward, the talkative new hire quickly blurted something out.

“Haha, actually... I’ve already got a friend working at Baekilmong.”

“No way.”

“Seriously?”

“Wow, which department?”

“Yeah. I think it was the research team?”

That comment caused an explosion of interest from the others!

Relieved that he had salvaged the mood, the new hire continued in a tone that walked the line between casual brag and forced modesty.

“But the funny thing is, they actually told me not to come here. Said I’d regret it or something.”

“Haha!”

“Friends who get hired first always say stuff like that.”

“Exactly!”

“...”

The others chuckled, as if they’d just heard the typical gripe about overwork in modern corporate life.

But then...

“...Department.”

“Sorry?”

The quiet one spoke.

“What department... did your friend say they were in?”

In that moment—

[Ah ah, mic test.]

“...!”

Everyone snapped their heads up at once.

[Oho! Looks like everyone’s gathered nicely. Then let’s begin~]

From a speaker mounted on the corner of the wall,

a man’s voice emerged—easygoing and upbeat.

[Welcome to all the new employees who’ve beaten 142-to-1 odds to get in! I’ll be in charge of today’s orientation. I’m Kwak Jaegang, Section Chief of Research Team 1.]

[How about we start with a round of applause, huh?]

Waaaah!

The new hires clapped cheerfully, though with a touch of puzzlement.

“Why through a speaker...?”

Why wasn’t anyone showing up in person?

“Hmm, maybe it’s something we’re meant to do on our own?”

[Ding ding ding! That’s correct!]

“...!”

He’s listening to us...!

[Yes, that’s right. Starting now, to build teamwork and corporate spirit, you new hires are going to take part in a very fun little activity~]

[It’s just a light mission—basically a recreation. There are even prizes!]

The new hires quickly interpreted this in a positive light.

“Guess it’s meant to help us bond quickly!”

“Honestly? Sounds great. Haha.”

After all, it was said that big-name corporations and hot venture startups often ran very creative or unusual orientations these days.

“Some companies even do murder mystery board games based on their company’s lore.”

[...Ahaha, now that sounds fun. This one’s similar. Of course! Now then, let’s...]

The voice from the speaker grew more excited in tone.

[Let’s begin the new employee orientation—starting now!]

At that exact moment—

Clickclickclickclick.

Lights flickered on in the annex lobby.

“...!”

Under the bright fluorescent lights, previously hidden things came into view.

Steel doors filled the dark hallway in the distance.

“...”

Somehow... rather than securing something, they looked more like they were built to seal something off. Heavy. Alien.

Why would an office annex have those kinds of doors...?

But before the question could even settle, everyone’s eyes were drawn to the brightest spot under the lights.

The main reception desk.

And sitting on top of it—something eye-catching.

“Ah...!”

The new hires blinked as they confirmed what it was.

A black, cylindrical box.

“A raffle drum?”

It was big. Old.

So worn and battered it looked entirely out of place in the sleek, marble-tiled annex lobby.

The thick paper drum had a lid covering the top, concealing its interior, but from the shape of the lid, they could make out three distinct holes.

Each circular cover was labeled with a number:

⓵ ⓶ ⓷

[Alright, come up one by one and draw a slip. You’ll draw in order, and then the next person follows, and so on.]

[Ah, let me show you an example. The three of you closest right now! Come up and draw one slip each, in order.]

“Ah, yes!”

Amid the murmurs, the three chosen stepped forward, a bit hesitant but unable to refuse.

They reached in and drew slips.

Flick.

Each slip bore a different instruction:

⓵ Basement 6

⓶ Room 322

⓷ Sang a lullaby

“What is this...”

[Now, turn your eyes back to the desk. See that whiteboard behind the raffle drum?]

Everyone looked over.

Printed text had been prepared on it.

I went down to Basement ⓵___

At the door of Room ⓶____

I ⓷______________.

[Just insert the results of each draw into the blanks. That creates the mission sentence! Simple, right? Haha!]

As the new hires placed the slips into their corresponding slots on the whiteboard, the sentence took shape.

Like this:

I went down to Basement 6

At the door of Room 322

I sang a lullaby.

“......”

“......”

There was something inexplicably eerie about the completed sentence.

‘Basement?’

It was at that moment—

[Ah, that’s right. For the team that performs best in their mission, an incredible reward awaits.]

As if reading the mood, the speaker’s voice delivered a cheerful incentive.

[It’s 100 points... usable like cash on the employee mall!]

One hundred points?

“Can we ask what kind of things you can buy with that?”

[Of course. That’s enough to buy a robot vacuum cleaner!]

“Oooh!”

If it was enough for a robot vacuum, then clearly it was a considerable amount!

The mood brightened considerably.

Rational thinking and a sense of realism returned along with the calculations.

Right, at worst it’s just a test of courage or something.

The company’s not stupid—there’s no way they’d actually make us do something dangerous.

Besides, with this many people around and teams of three, it wasn’t even that scary.

The other new hires had escaped safely.

He was right.

The smoke was quietly pooled at their feet.

Like a scarecrow.

That bizarre entity.

Wait a second.

Yes. Maybe he wasn’t something to be afraid of. Maybe he was safe!

Unable to endure the situation any longer—the fact that he was trapped in an elevator with something so horrific—he clung to rationalization and turned his head.

He locked eyes with the Security Team officer.

But then...

“Thank... you...”

Had it always... looked this melted?

The officer’s arm was disturbingly long. Something was wrong... too many feet. Its melted lower body was bulging and twitching, fused into the smoke across the elevator floor.

And it had thirty-seven eyes.

Eyes that didn’t blink, staring directly at him.

“U-uh...uhhh...”

What... is that?

What is that?!

[Now arriving: Basement Level 11.]

The elevator’s movement delayed the onset of panic.

They had arrived again.

On Basement 11, an employee in a doctor’s coat was conducting a pleasant little pre-op consultation in Room 910.

Hhhshhh.

Soon, the bizarre entity in the doctor’s coat became a bloodless corpse, its limbs stitched together in the wrong places.

Because the Security Team officer’s melted lower body had spread across it.

Duty of an Employee

Lunatic.

Getting involved with others in a situation like this was a good way to die. Self-preservation was the only path to survival...

Be grateful for your luck

[B13]

I was alone again.

Something pulsed.

Like perfume from a broken bottle.

Ding.

Dark hallway. Flickering red and blue lights. Eerie iron doors.

The security team.

Suspicion

I knew who this was.

Kwak Jaegang.

Apparently unbothered by the hostile stares of the security team, he cheerfully pulled documents from his coat and flipped through them, beaming at me.

Just now.

Chapter 212

A familiar name.

Something I had known.

It surged up from deep within me again like a rising tide, but—

I did not react.

“Employee?”

Because I knew it would be unwise to express this feeling.

......

Why is that?

Suddenly, a strange sensation began to stir inside my head.

It felt like trying to forcibly pull up an anchor that had sunk into the deep sea.

With my previous senses eroded, my crushed brain and consciousness strained to resume thinking like they once did.

Jang Heowoon...

He was dead.

His body had lain collapsed on the resort room floor.

But... that is to say... he had been reconstructed, contaminated, and brought back out as a resort employee.

And I had...

I gave him the wish ticket.

If the wish ticket I passed on to Jang Heowoon had really been delivered successfully, and he had come back to life as a result—

If what I saw was him living again, as a human being—

......

My broken mind reached a conclusion.

That this was relief—something I only realized very, very slowly.

A direct emotion, felt for the first time in far too long, landed like a dull ache.

“How did hearing their names make you feel? Lee Jeongin, Park Yonghae...... Jang Heowoon.”

And I understood that telling this to the notorious researcher in front of me would never be a wise choice.

The threads of that judgment barely tied themselves together in my head.

“What do you think?”

I stared at him in silence.

Kwak Jaegang, eyes full of anticipation, finally clicked his tongue when silence continued to stretch.

“Another ‘no response,’ is it? As expected, memories from sacrificial mediums used as conduits don’t mean much... Haha, my apologies. But, you know, as a man of science I just have to ask these things. Hahaha!”

Kwak Jaegang chuckled to himself awkwardly, then muttered as if to no one.

“Still, I did get quite a show today, employee. You haven’t reacted to anything like a proper machine for the past half year...”

......

Half a year?

“They say doing a fun little event like this every six months helps prevent burnout. Don’t you agree?”

That’s when I realized.

It had only been half a year since I was thrown down here to Basement 13.

Or rather... it had already been half a year.

“Wow~ must’ve been fun. You even helped those three-person newbie teams—so obviously fresh-faced—explore the sublevels of the annex!”

Silence.

Silence.

......

......

“Did you maybe feel like it? Driven by a sense of duty to help others?”

......

More precisely—at the old television mounted in the ceiling corner.

The one where the employee training video used to play.

Employee Regulation 17: Cooperate with your fellow employees.

The sentence from the training video.

Even the font was perfectly replicated, and Kwak Jaegang recognized it instantly.

“Aha, of course!”

The frantic tapping of a stylus on his tablet echoed in the room.

“So it was because of the training video. Very... haha, well—shall we say gentle? Or perhaps benevolent? Or maybe it just means that discipline and order are key values! I see. A special department employee, indeed... haha, I understand completely.”

Then his pen stopped, and his voice dropped to something more insinuating.

“But you wasted your effort, you know. Those participants you helped...”

He raised both hands in a mock surprise gesture.

“They weren’t fellow employees. Haha!”

......

I know.

“They weren’t new hires. They were just test subjects!”

It hadn’t been an actual orientation for a field expedition team to begin with.

It was an experimental project inspired by that structure, borrowing its name.

Experiment Title: New Employee Orientation

They were never real new employees.

Strictly speaking, they were applicants who had applied for a position but were rejected for various reasons.

Then a fake email was sent to trick them into thinking they had been accepted into their intended department.

The reason was...

“Think of them like part-time hires gathered for specimen collection!”

To supplement civilian exploration records.

An effort designed to collect meaningful new data from the Darkness by using untrained civilians, whose cases had been lacking in past reports.

Rejected applicants with suitable aptitudes were selected and placed into a simulation that mimicked the real new employee orientation process.

The company couldn’t just carelessly deploy its Darkness reserves into the civilian world.

That’s why most of its exploration records came from the Darkness Exploration Team—skilled professionals.

Even new hires, who were the closest thing to civilians, only entered controlled orientations using Darkness specimens curated to test specific skill sets.

Then one researcher had a brilliant idea:

Let’s recycle the applicants!

There was no need to gather participants in secret or dodge legal oversight. The timing was perfect for easy recruitment.

And of course, it wasn’t entirely a scam.

There was a reward that could justify the deception.

Rejected applicants who successfully completed the mission would be given a special contract offer to join the Darkness Exploration Team.

Thus, the company’s official justification was this:

“To offer a second chance at employment through supplemental acceptance.”

In other words...

“Well, you might run into them again later—as actual colleagues!”

Anyone who completed their mission and survived would be granted an employment opportunity, which, in a way, made it no different from a real orientation.

And I know.

Jang Heowoon passed.

.......

Could it really be Jang Heowoon?

“Hm. Would you like to meet him again?”

WARNING 1

WARNING 2

Reason: Work request outside of contracted hours

Smoke began rising at my feet.

I knew it.

Knowing when to stop.

Some time later.

I awoke not in the sublevels of the annex, not in Basement 13, but somewhere else.

A new worksite.

Kwak Jaegang typed feverishly, euphoria rising all the way to the top of his head.

Recording experimental results was pure joy.

Especially when the experiment involved such a massive number of test subjects and yielded anomalous results through multi-faceted interactions!

“Hahaha, I can’t believe this actually happened. How in the world did anyone manage to sign a contract with something like that...”

Eyes glinting, he stared into the PC screen.

Displayed there was a cleanly transcribed version of his handwritten notes: a simplified experiment record.

New Employee Orientation – 21st Attempt

Listed were case studies involving interactions between orientation participants and the security officers of each sublevel of the annex—the so-called “Special Department Employees.”

And without a doubt, the longest entry:

2.2.1 Basement 13 Security Officer (hereafter referred to as 130666)

“To think I’d meet it again like this.”

He’d harbored quiet hopes the moment Director Cheong first assigned him a “special task.”

But for it to really show up!

Kwak Jaegang recalled the entity that had once interrogated him—a strange being of horns, gas lamps, and dense smoke.

That bizarre thing which had borrowed the vessel of the fastest-promoted agent in the Exploration Team.

Of course, the version he saw now seemed more mechanical, more bound than before...

But wasn’t that all the more reason to be curious?

Did some kind of side effect manifest?

Truly fascinating!

As he analyzed the emerging behavioral trends from this experiment, his excitement only grew.

He glanced at the note he’d attached:

This subject, while serving as Basement 13’s security officer for 179 days, has adhered strictly to employee regulations, showing none of the non-cooperative or aggressive anomalies typically observed in Special Department entities.

However, in the current experiment, the subject identified a regulatory loophole, boarded the elevator stopped in their jurisdiction, and accompanied incoming test subjects—proactively aiding their survival.

This is the thing.

Said behavior resembles the pre-death dream-fragment collection tendencies of the employee corpse used during the signing of 130666’s employment contract.

Suspected to be indirect ego influence—further experimentation pending request.

“This is 100% getting censored.”

Anything labeled as a researcher’s personal opinion might get flagged—or the entire section might just be deleted.

It wasn’t all that shocking that a Security Team Special Department contract had required the complete sacrificial offering of an employee’s corpse—but the identity was the problem.

“Would they really want to admit that it used to be the elite employee with the fastest promotion record?”

They’d never want to name specifics.

Especially if some lunatic in the research team managed to deduce it on their own without being told...

Kwak Jaegang shrugged, thinking of that intriguing employee—Kim Soleum—and let his fingers fly even faster over the keys.

Because the next part was even more interesting!

130666’s Anomalous Aggression

: When enveloped by the subject’s smoke (hereafter referred to as 130666-1), humanoid Darkness entities are transformed into corpses.

No signs of life. Blood and cell samples confirm early-stage decomposition consistent with human cadavers.

CCTV footage of the elevator while 130666 was active intermittently shows the entire space engulfed in 130666, resembling paranormal phenomena.

He hummed as he recalled the annex basement security staff who had turned into corpses.

They were being slowly restored by the Darkness, but appeared to be undergoing bizarre, chaotic mutations as if suffering from some residual side effect.

Even Security Team members exhibited unusual reactions just by getting close to the affected area—some form of contamination flare-up, perhaps.

Kwak Jaegang grinned wide.

“Really, such fun little friends.”

...And so.

No additional risk anomalies observed. 130666 returns to employee form prior to disembarking the elevator.

And therefore—

Therefore, subject is officially approved as fit for full Special Department duty.

“Hahahaha!”

However—

Dedicated supervisory personnel will be assigned to prevent spontaneous incidents or fatal safety accidents.

Kwak Jaegang was thrilled.

This had already been approved at the upper levels.

Safety Lead: Security Team 3 Captain (J3, legal name: ■■■)

Control Support: Special Oversight Division Agent (Badger, legal name: Park Minseong)

“...Ah.”

130666, in its newly reassigned worksite, was about to meet two Security Team employees.

Chapter 213

Before I opened my eyes,

I heard a voice echoing in my head.

Instructions.

—You have been reassigned to a new post. Follow the researcher’s orders. You have been reassigned to a new post. Follow the researcher’s orders...

The first thing I saw was a mask.

Not a face melting into mosaic, but a laminated paper mask covering it.

But I knew that animal.

“I’ll try to be as non-disruptive as possible... I go by Badger.”

Badger.

This person, wearing a Security Team uniform—I knew him.

Deputy Park Minseong of Team D.

My senior.

And he didn’t recognize me.

“Today’s just a simple introduction...”

Of course he wouldn’t.

There was nothing about my current state that resembled the former Kim Soleum, aside from physical measurements—and he had never seen this body before.

A different voice.

J3.

Employment contract

“...You said you were quitting.”

New employment contract:

Permanent position

He remembered.

The mutual acquaintance between them.

The one who had safely quit this company not long ago.

The one who’d gone silent since.

I didn’t respond.

Clack.

The metal door locked behind him.

Breath catching.

It came from the Badger mask.

Disturbed by the emotional agitation, the contamination symptoms briefly surged to the surface, only to be suppressed again beneath the mask.

More than 40 seconds passed before the Badger mask could shake off the identity of the ■■ Kindergarten teacher he had once merged with.

“I-It’ll be okay.”

Footsteps approached me.

Not possible

“...!”

Contamination means a former human being has been transformed by a supernatural phenomenon.

Recovery from contamination means restoring the original human’s body and mind.

And in my case—

“Roe Deer.”

Not possible

—I no longer had the former.

......

For some reason, I didn’t want to act.

It didn’t.

“No rejection? No confusion? That’s normal, you know. It’s expected. So...”

Atypical responses to one’s original name were a signature symptom of contamination.

It didn’t apply to me.

“Soleum...”

Clack.

As he said it again, Deputy Park Minseong flinched and suddenly gripped his own wrist.

The Security Team-issued control watch.

Tick, tick.

The sound of the clock ticking.

And then—I could faintly hear a warning that hadn’t been audible before, now shared with me due to our similar contract parameters.

—A violation of safety protocol has been detected. Time remaining until disciplinary detention reduced by 7 seconds. 71 seconds, 70 seconds, 69 seconds...

A system announcement, just like the one that rang in my own head.

A regulation imposed on contaminated Security Team members to control their speech.

He must’ve realized he’d violated the safety rule: referring to a contaminated coworker by their original name during duty.

Thus, I witnessed a specific operational mechanism—one never spelled out in the official Darkness Exploration Logs.

What mattered was—

—You have been reassigned to a new post. Follow the researcher’s orders.

It was work.

“......”

“......”

What should I do now?

In the silence, I finally began to examine my surroundings.

Rusty iron door. Sealed space. Resembled Room 666 of Basement 13 Annex, but it wasn’t the same.

Still, I could recognize where I was.

—Baekilmong Corporation Headquarters – Underground Isolation Chamber

A place I’d visited when I was still human, during a disciplinary cleaning.

An isolation corridor accessible only via the Security Team’s equipment storage—beyond it, the sealed cells...

But I could tell.

This was far deeper than the chamber I’d been taken to after escaping the “Hungry Hangman” Darkness.

Far deeper than the one I’d cleaned under disciplinary orders. A chamber located in the very core isolation zone.

My instincts as a worker told me so.

And the layout of this room—

I ran a gloved finger along the desk lamp.

Lamp. Wooden desk. Bed. Towel.

I knew this arrangement.

“Company housing.”

Identical to the room I had stayed in when I was Employee Kim Soleum.

Everything matched, down to the obsessive detail.

It felt almost ritualistic. Ceremonial.

As if designed to appease some part of the Darkness bound by my employment contract. To keep it from acting out.

At the same time, I could feel someone was watching—expecting a reaction from me.

This must be Kwak Jaegang’s doing.

Exactly the kind of thing he would orchestrate.

I brushed the desk again with my gloved fingers.

Should I be angry?

My melted and flattened brain strained to think the way it used to, but it was difficult...

So this means... I’m no longer in charge of Basement 13 Annex. My new post is security for the Headquarters Isolation Chambers?

That wasn’t hard to understand.

But in that case—

That person wasn’t necessary.

Job description requested

“...Your job is to work with Roe Deer.”

Inappropriate form of address

Reason: Informal designation

“...Ah.”

The one wearing the Badger mask turned their head, then let out a small sound when they saw the CCTV in the corner.

The CCTV seemed to lack audio functionality—likely to prevent contamination—but the gaze from beyond it could still be felt.

They’re watching.

And there was no reason to provide unnecessary assurance to the assigned researcher about my current condition.

Perhaps noticing the warning I’d written in smoke, the Badger’s tone shifted.

“Then... what should I call you? Haha, let’s just say we’re coming up with another nickname. You already called me Roe Deer before...”

An animal I like.

Unknown

“.......”

......

Job description requested

: include concrete examples

What are they holding?

“...!”

A syringe-like object.

An injector.

For subduing Special Department employees

“Don’t worry. I won’t use it. Really. Really, I won’t...”

Why?

Use recommended in appropriate scenarios

▶ When bipedal form collapses

▶ When extreme anxiety is detected

▶ When extreme anxiety is detected

Criteria: Subjective emotional judgment of handler

“...Haha...”

A choked sound came from behind the Badger mask.

“You’re the same as ever, aren’t you?”

......

“Yeah... It’s going to be fine.”

And somehow, as if reassured, their trembling and movements slowly calmed.

They reached out as if to pat my shoulder—but lowered their hand halfway through.

......

Clack.

“Captain, we—”

The door opened, and the Security Captain entered.

One might think he had walked out earlier due to shock, but his physical reactions showed no sign of rejection.

If anything, he strode in without hesitation, startling the one in the Badger mask.

Then he walked up to me and extended his left arm.

“Here.”

In his outstretched hand were small, round objects.

Nostalgia Candy.

I can’t eat it.

I don’t have a mouth. I don’t know where in this body a mouth would even be.

Maybe I never made one—because I didn’t need it.

I only believed I had one.

More importantly... I can’t remove the gas mask.

Not possible

Wish Ticket

I saw his face.

...!

What is this?

Why can I see it?

Even his voice is clear. I can distinguish it. I can recognize it! What is this? Why? Could there be another truth I’ve failed to perceive...

......

Ah.

I understand now.

“Why is the smoke...!”

Because this person was closer to a ghost story than a human being.

The Security Captain’s contamination was so profound and entrenched that even intensive removal through the Security Team curriculum couldn’t prevent him from existing at the boundary of reality.

This means nothing.

“I guess you’re not... in pain.”

And yet, despite that—

“Then it’s okay...”

That strange, dull ache I’d felt in the elevator began to slowly return.

The Security Captain stepped into the smoke without hesitation, raised his hand with a somewhat clumsy motion... and patted my back.

As if it had been a very long time since he’d last done so.

“...You’ll be alright.”

......

I inhaled the smoke. The swirling black tendrils on the floor were drawn back into me.

The Security Captain glanced briefly at the Nostalgia Candy in his hand.

“Well then, I’ll go fetch something else...”

I shook my head.

The Nostalgia Series is unnecessary for work.

What I need now is something else.

—It is now your working hours. Please rise. It is now your working hours. Please rise...

It’s time.

Requesting work briefing

“Ah, that’s...”

[Aha, so eager to get started!]

The voice came from the corner CCTV.

The Security Captain stepped in front of me.

I shook my head at the Security Captain. But he stood firm, unmoving.

Only Kwak Jaegang’s voice echoed loudly through the sealed chamber.

Chapter 214

[On rainy days. Like today.]

Rain was drizzling outside the window.

Crash.

Thunder echoed like tinnitus before fading away.

Nerves frayed, the two office workers in suits had just entered the house on the outskirts of Seoul and were being shown to the sofa.

“Please wait here a moment.”

“Yes. Thank you.”

Hoo.

Lee Jinyeong, a mid-level employee in Baekilmong Corporation’s Sales Team—one of their so-called “delivery staff”—exhaled as he adjusted his tie.

The middle-aged woman who had let them into the house and said she would “announce their arrival” was already halfway up the stairs, only her back visible.

Probably not a family member of the client.

She must be the housekeeper.

He couldn’t imagine someone wealthy enough to commission custom pharmaceuticals from Baekilmong scrubbing floors or washing dishes themselves.

Still, buying the medicine personally is surprising.

He glanced sideways at the box his superior was holding.

They were here to present and sell a special potion.

Only after joining the company had he learned that Baekilmong Corporation didn’t just develop hair-loss solutions—it also sold far more exotic pharmaceuticals.

And that their ingredients were... not exactly common.

Some kind of human-derived compound... hopefully not that.

It smelled slightly illegal and made him uneasy. But then again, what major corporation in modern society wasn’t skating the edge of legality? His job was to stay uninvolved, do his work, and collect his paycheck.

...Even if something felt off.

That’s sales for you...

This was his first time doing a regular delivery to this particular house.

His superior had brought him in to replace someone who was on leave.

“Deputy Manager, are you all right?”

“Mm. Not great.”

The company man, looking pale and ill—maybe due to digestive issues—replied gloomily.

They said this client wasn’t difficult...

Was it the rain?

Jinyeong recalled the conversation they'd had before coming here.

About today’s regular delivery client.

—They're the type who’ll break a little sweat, but you can handle it... It’s just, the house feels off when it rains.

—Feels off?

—Yeah. You know that sense you get in a place with bad energy? Like when you’re near a tunnel where an accident happened?

—Hmm...

—I even brought a talisman, just in case.

That extreme?

Maybe he’s just not feeling well himself.

Anyone would feel a little down in weather this damp and gloomy.

The house looks fine to me.

He shrugged internally as he looked around the high-end townhouse in Seoul.

It was luxurious and pristine.

Clearly cleaned by someone else. He felt a small pang of envy as he looked down at the spotless floor.

And then he saw it.

...Huh?

There was something shabby under the sofa.

He reached out without thinking and picked it up...

Thin, smooth texture.

...Sketchbook paper?

The kind a child might use. He flipped it over absently...

I’m hungry

The writing looked like a child’s scrawl—awkward, wobbly letters.

Around the words, crude crayon drawings of stick-figure people, all circles and lines, filled the space.

Maybe it was meant to be a family portrait?

“Hey, they’re coming.”

“Ah.”

Jinyeong set the paper down beside the sofa and stood to greet the clients descending the stairs.

“Good afternoon!”

“Yes. Nice to meet you.”

The couple spoke with unexpectedly soft voices.

“You’re here for a delivery?”

“That’s right!”

His superior switched expressions smoothly and greeted them with practiced ease.

“It’s your usual C-grade delivery.”

He handed over the sealed box. The couple opened it, confirmed the bottle filled with light green liquid, and nodded in satisfaction.

What the hell is that stuff?

Unfamiliar with the inner workings of Baekilmong’s potions, the sales staffer just snuck glances.

Was it some kind of youth elixir?

Whatever it was, payment wasn’t collected on-site. Rumor had it, it wasn’t paid in money at all.

They say it’s repaid by “helping the company’s work”...

Maybe covering up something illegal, or rigging a bid—Jinyeong could only guess.

Of course, he never said a word.

As always with regular deliveries, the next step was smoothing things over.

Maintaining a good rapport so the contract would continue.

And, as usual, he fumbled for some idle small talk.

“Oh, does your child like to draw?”

“...Pardon?”

“They left this behind.”

Recalling the picture, he reached toward the sofa. It had been right there...

“A crayon drawing, said they were hungry—”

...What?

He blinked.

All he felt was the leather of the bare sofa.

Weird. He definitely left it right here...

“What paper?”

“Ah.”

Turning back, he saw the couple looking at him, puzzled.

“Our oldest already moved out. He’s a college student.”

“I... see.”

Their expressions said, What nonsense are you talking about? We don’t have a child here.

Shit.

They stood abruptly, saying they’d go store the potion. As soon as they left the room, the superior turned and gave him a what-the-hell-is-wrong-with-you glare.

What the hell was going on?

Like that.

As if they were staring out from the page.

Ignorance.

Dark.

When a house goes dark suddenly, people usually react—talk, fumble around, make noise.

Rattletattletattletattle...

Something eerie.

...That smell of rain.

The scent of damp earth.

Not a smell you’d expect in a well-maintained home. The junior employee took shallow breaths and stepped forward, discomfort prickling at the back of his neck.

Was that the talisman?

No.

In the opposite direction from the staircase.

Too late.

bathroombathtub

Because he heard footsteps.

Coming down from upstairs.

Getting closer.

Too late.

What is this?

His superior’s talisman.

That was...

...SOS!

His panicked brain cracked the code instantly.

Where does it connect to?

Maybe talisman hadn’t meant a literal charm—but a means of calling for help.

Clinging to that hope like a lifeline, Jinyeong frantically reached out and mashed what looked like the send button.

“Please, please...”

And then—

[Request in progress.]

“...!”

[Would you like to contact the Security Team? Additional charges will apply.]

“Yes! Yes!!”

He didn’t care what it cost. He needed help. Now!

[Request in progress.]

Jinyeong babbled incoherently, explaining everything—this bizarre house, the rain, the picture, the lie.

The child!

“The child who drew that...!”

“AAAGHH!”

The beeper clattered to the ground.

And from the shadow cast where it landed—another black shadow began to rise.

[Request complete]

[Security Team dispatched]

Employees in full Baekilmong Corporation Security Team uniforms began to appear.

Chapter 215

The Security Team agents raised their eyes.

Into the darkness of the pantry.

They emerged from beneath the shadows using their transport gear, and now locked eyes with the sales employee who was lying on the floor, holding his breath.

“A child... No.”

The one in the animal mask drawn like a child’s picture—a badger—calmed his own agitated contamination.

Soon, a flat, professional voice followed.

“Employee of Sales Team 4.”

“Y-yes...”

“Identity confirmed. We will now begin the expense calculation.”

The sales employee swallowed hard.

Covered head to toe in specialized black gear, faces obscured, outfitted with bizarre instruments—they looked like urban legends made flesh.

They were unmistakably supernatural operatives assigned to a special task. And yet, their presence brought momentary relief.

Of course, fear crept in again just moments later—this situation was still beyond comprehension.

“Um, what should I... do?”

As he darted his eyes around, trying to assess the situation, something caught his attention.

One of the newly arrived Security Team agents was holding a black carrier cage.

A steel cage, like one meant for trapping animals.

...Wait a second.

“Y-you’re not going to... put me in there, are you?”

“No, of course not.”

The badger agent gently stroked the carrier.

The sales employee had just begun to relax.

“There’s already someone in it.”

“...What?”

“Don’t overthink it.”

Seeing the sales agent visibly crack under the strain of the incomprehensible, the badger gave a bitter smile—just for himself.

And then—

“Cramped...”

J3, who was holding the carrier with one hand, showed little interest in the entire situation.

It had been ages since he’d been “out” for a job not wearing his regular guard uniform.

Then again, out wasn’t the right word for a space this cramped.

Still, the badger, uncharacteristically kind for a Security Team agent, made a clinical announcement.

“We’ve confirmed all the reported details. We’ll now begin the oper—”

“Who’s there?”

“Who is it?”

“...!”

Tatatatatata.

It sounded like raindrops hitting a window, but the scraping noise from the door grated at the nerves.

It was fingernails.

Ten fingertips were scratching the door.

“There’s someone else.”

“Someone else is here.”

Whispered like a breath.

The voices were disturbingly close. Because—

They were pressing their faces against the crack in the door to whisper.

“All too late now.”

“Too late, it’s all too late.”

The couple was peering inside.

Inside the very door they had locked themselves.

The sales employee froze, chilled to the very top of his scalp.

But then...

“Are they human?”

“Hard to say...”

Surprisingly calm conversation came from the “Security Team” members beside him.

“Maybe they were... once?”

J3, as a reflex, tried adjusting the brim of his nonexistent security cap—then settled for his mask.

From behind it, a dim gleam like an old animal’s eye showed through.

He gently set the carrier cage down on the pantry floor.

Right next to the sales employee.

“Hey... would you be okay waiting here for a bit...?”

“M-me?”

But those words weren’t meant for the sales employee.

“You’ll be okay...?”

Tap.

The iron grate of the black carrier gave a soft rattle.

As if the thing inside was answering.

“We’ll be right back. Just wait a little...”

Tap.

Another reply came.

J3 rested his hand lightly on the cage and then stood up.

“Listen... if you don’t want to get hurt... stay close to this.”

That part was directed at the sales employee.

“Whatever you do... don’t touch it carelessly.”

“......”

The employee nodded, swallowing hard. He pressed up against the side of the black cage.

He could tell, somehow, that this was meant to protect him.

...An uncanny cold leaked from the carrier.

“Then, we’ll begin.”

Leaving the sales employee behind, the two Security Team agents moved to the pantry door and forced it open.

“...!!”

Their bodies blocked the view outside, but the sound of the rain had grown fiercer. The sales employee shrank into himself as much as possible. He caught a glimpse of the Security Team stepping out—

Then the door shut again, and the rain sound faded.

Thud.

.......

Screeeeech, screeeeech, rip.

The noise of something being torn echoed violently.

The sales employee instinctively clutched the cage tighter. He tried not to imagine what was happening outside.

The cold kept his sanity intact.

Inside the cage, some kind of smoke—or something like it—seemed to slither and stir.

Then, after what felt like an unbearably long moment—

Boom.

“...!”

The thunderous noise stopped. The rain stopped too.

The door opened.

And the two Security Team agents were calmly standing there, checking their equipment.

“Target neutralized.”

“......!”

They were... unharmed.

A faint glimmer of hope welled up in the sales employee’s chest, even as his knees buckled.

So now he could leave?

He rose shakily and looked toward the hallway.

And that’s when he understood what neutralized had meant.

“Ngkh.”

He choked back a scream.

The hallway was wet.

Soaked, not just from water—but from bodily fluids splashed like blood across the floor and wallpaper, giving everything a dark, sticky sheen.

And on the wall directly across—

The couple, contorted and fixed in place, their limbs bent at impossible angles.

“...”

Every movable joint crushed and pinned down like a twisted centipede—each limb nailed with colorful pushpins, like a grotesque child’s craft project.

An overwhelming force. Brutality incarnate.

And those school-supply pushpins.

Every hole in their faces leaked fluid... like his dead superior had.

But even less human.

“......”

“Oh, right. This was a collection assignment.”

Collection?

“Starting with intel?”

“Yeah...”

The dried-out, sluggish Security Team man approached the thing that used to be the couple—now nailed to the wall.

He removed a pushpin from their mouth.

“...!”

“Hey... is there... a kid here?”

One of the things the sales employee had screamed into the beeper.

His heart chilled.

What made it worse—

The couple’s mouths opened and began to speak.

“P-please... please help... sob,”

“Answer the question first.”

Suddenly—

Tears streamed down from their eyes.

“Th-the child is... in the bathroom.”

“You’re trapped here now too. It’s too late... please...”

“......”

The Security Team agent—referred to as the Captain—just stared at them.

“What’s upstairs?”

“There’s something evil. Don’t go. No, you have to run, now.”

“There might still be time. If you hurry, maybe you can—”

“Hm...”

As if debating, the Security Team agent raised a hand toward them.

And in the next instant—

“...Done.”

Crack.

A clear, unmistakable sound.

He broke their necks.

Both of them—one hand on each—snapped and crushed with an inhuman level of force, twisting their heads around.

Effortlessly.

.......

They killed them?

“Oh, you got everything you needed?”

“Yep.”

“Understood. ...Caller, you should be fine now. Just hand that over.”

His hands shook.

Something’s wrong.

That’s not something people do...

The sketchbook paper.

With new words written on it.

scared

Truly—

monster

Right beside the pantry.

The place the paper had identified as the answer from the very start.

bathroombathtub

The bathroom.

“No good.”

He was caught by the head.

A beast’s forepaw, with countless claws.

“Hold still...”

He blinked. The fear of death froze him solid.

I’m going to die.

“Teacher, relax your hand! A perfect teacher obeys the rules given by a fellow teacher!”

His hand went limp. The sketchbook paper slipped away.

No!

“Shall I read it?”
