The sales employee moved only his eyes. Run. Find a way to run. The front door, the living room, upstairs, a window...

Ah.

Right.

The window!

Why hadn’t he thought of that before? If the front door was death, the window might be salvation.

I have to run. Now.

He struggled wildly, crawling toward the window. He kicked, clawed, pushed at the beast’s paw—but every effort failed.

Still, he couldn’t give up—

“If you go there... you’ll die.”

......

Huh?

“Stay put...”

“W-why would I d-die...”

“Mm. Because you’d be... closer to the origin, maybe...?”

Origin?

He turned his head.

“You should remember—you said it yourself.”

What?

“When you reported it. That this house felt strange sometimes...”

“......”

He stopped moving.

And recalled one of the things he’d shouted into the pager.

Something his superior had said—

“The house feels off when it rains.”

The sales employee looked at the window.

Outside, the rain was still falling softly...

......

“Rainy days... right...”

The slow, lethargic voice crawled into his ear.

“If you open a black umbrella indoors, something comes in... If you leave your wet shoes upside down, a ghost wears them...”

“Captain, is that true?”

“No...”

The Security Captain stared at the badger, eyes devoid of reflection.

Then he continued, slowly.

“Most of those are... just nonsense...”

“Oh.”

“But sometimes... they say... rumors like that create things...”

Darkness.

The word he didn’t say aloud—the one the sales employee, once a field survey team lead, silently filled in.

The Captain’s explanation wasn’t over.

“And there’s this old story...”

“......”

“If you’re holding an umbrella in the rain, and you hear a whisper from beyond it... never respond...”

......

“Because it’s not a person.”

The sales employee locked eyes with the Security Captain.

“Hey... hasn’t someone been... talking to you all along...?”

At that moment—

“Deputy Lee?”

The sales employee froze.

“Deputy Lee, are you in there?”

A familiar voice from the front door.

Knock knock knock.

They were knocking.

“Deputy Lee, open the door. What the hell is going on?”

It was...

“Why aren’t you coming out?”

...the voice of his dead superior.

Knock knock knock knock.

The corpse that had collapsed at the front entrance, neck snapped, was now gone.

Instead, all that could be heard beyond the rain was someone knocking behind the front door...

Along with the superior’s voice.

“Come on, open up. You need to come out too.”

The strength drained from the sales employee’s body.

The Security Captain released his grip on his head. With a thud, the sales employee sank to the floor, dazed and confused.

...Wow.

The badger, watching this unfold, was quietly impressed.

He had just realized the depth of the Captain’s darkness-worn expertise—hidden beneath his apathetic tone.

It was something that clever newbie in Squad D might’ve grown into if he’d stayed in the Field Survey Team for a few more years...

No. That was a meaningless thought. After all, they were both already part of the Security Team. Nearly rude of him to compare. The badger swallowed his thoughts bitterly.

Meanwhile, the voices multiplied.

“There’s someone here! Are you okay?”

“Oh dear, this old man fell down... Could you help me?”

“...Wait a second, didn’t the grandmother of this house die a few years ago? Don’t listen! Something’s wrong!”

From upstairs, the master bedroom, beyond the side door of the kitchen—voices were coming from everywhere. As if everyone who’d been in the house was now shaking off the blackout at once.

They drew closer.

With the sound of the rain.

“Where are you?”

“...! Don’t respond to the voice upstairs. No one’s there right now...!”

“Be careful. I think something’s wrong here...”

Footsteps.

And then—

“Oh, the door opened.”

......

“I’m coming in. Where are you?”

Even the dead superior.

“Uhhkk.”

What the hell is this? Which ones are real?

Which one of them is the monster?

The sales employee tried to mentally review everything he’d heard and distinguish them, but the badger seized him.

“Agh!”

“Stay calm. You’re all righ—Captain?”

While the situation screamed for panic, the Security Captain casually knelt down and pressed his head against the carrier cage.

Speaking to whatever was inside.

“What do you think...? Does it seem right to you...?”

Tap.

“Hm. Then... shall I do it?”

Tap tap.

“...Still...”

Tap tap.

“......”

The Captain reluctantly stepped aside, slowly, touching his mask.

One tap for yes. Two taps for no.

Seeing the familiar signal, the badger gave a bitter smile beneath his concealed face.

He really hasn’t changed.

“...Then I’ll open it.”

He approached the carrier and released the locking mechanism.

Clank.

The grate dropped and the door to the cage opened...

Smoke spilled across the floor.

And from the void inside the carrier, a single intact leg stepped out.

“...!”

A black leg, clad in black tactical boots.

Identical uniform to the other Security Team agents—but what emerged from the dense black smoke was a horned, inhuman figure.

A tall man.

Something it was hard to believe had ever fit inside that crate.

“U-uhh...”

The sales employee met the creature’s yellow eyes.

Recommendation: Do Not Stare

▶ Lower your gaze

▶ Close your eyes

He dropped his eyes instantly.

Instinct beyond panic.

Don’t keep looking.

Cold sweat ran down the back of his neck.

But the text—formed from smoke—still followed him.

Recommendation: Do Not Stare

▶ Lower your gaze ⋁ : Correct

▶ Close your eyes

Like praise.

And then—

The sound returned. The same tearing noise from earlier.

SSSSCCRREEEECH!

The wet splatter of fluid. Weeping noises.

The Security Team was neutralizing what was approaching.

“How are they telling them apart...?”

Humans from monsters.

Or maybe—

Maybe they’re not distinguishing at all.

The sales employee curled up. His heart thundered, threatening to burst from his chest. He felt like he was going to die...

Then the smoke-lettered text shifted.

As if responding to his inner voice.

Select the dangerous one.

▶ The couple

▶ The sketchbook paper

You want me to decide?

He answered reflexively.

“Th-the couple!”

Look at them—these ghost-things talking and creeping closer. No doubt the couple had been ghosts too. They had to be!

▶ The couple ⋁ : Correct

Of course.

Ah, maybe anyone who spoke in a human voice was dangerous? Is that it?

“The couple was the threat, so don’t be afraid of us while we neutralize them”...

Was that the message?

If so, it made sense. Real logic.

The employee regained a sliver of composure and recalled what he should be doing.

I must have panicked... this must’ve been trying to calm me down.

These Security Team agents—they could talk rationally. If they really had come to rescue him, if someone—or something—was still trapped in this house, like the “little one”...

Then he should say something more. About the sketchbook paper that had guided him.

He licked his dry lips and opened his mouth.

“Th-the sketchbook paper...”

And then—

▶ The sketchbook paper ⋁ : Correct

“......”

Huh?

He looked again.

It hadn’t changed.

Select the dangerous one.

▶ The couple ⋁ : Correct

▶ The sketchbook paper ⋁ : Correct

So this meant...

“Dangerous...”

Both are dangerous.

“......Why?”

Same

His mouth opened slightly as he absently mouthed the meaning.

“Then what was I supposed to believe in? I mean... what was I supposed to trust?”

None

“......”

On rainy days

Don’t answer

When strangers speak to you.

Ah.

Recommendation: Do Not Stare – Ended

The sales employee raised his head.

But the black-clad man was already gone.

“......”

Only a wisp of black smoke remained—what he’d been speaking to.

Like a magician showing a distraction before an injection. A misdirection.

To prevent an outburst.

“......”

The tearing noises had stopped. Around him, the bodies of those fixed like the couple, silenced by the Security Team.

Too dark to see clearly—small mercy.

And... the black smoke.

The sales employee watched it drift away, dissipating like steam.

It floated past him, toward a door just beside the pantry—

The only place from which sound still came.

The bathroom.

From inside, a chorus of horrific screams was now erupting.

I looked at the bathtub in front of me.

Inside, the water echoed with the sound of rain, filled to the brim.

A transparent, deep pool, ready to devour any victim drawn in by the voices.

Like scratching in my head, words emerged—

[Rainwater Echo]

A superstition-modernized “Power” of the Nameless Shining Sect, asserting that whispers on rainy days are the voice of ghosts. Sect affiliation: Imitator Clan.

Targets victims who approach the pool of rainwater on rainy days. During this process, it mimics the appearance and voices of those previously consumed, luring new prey.

Expands the meaning of “speaking” to include letters, notes, drawings, and more—disturbingly literal and broad.

Presents a binary ghost-story format, forcing victims to choose whom to trust. But no matter the choice, it’s a trap—both paths lead to ruin.

Exploits the human hope that “choosing correctly” will lead to survival.

But—

Due to the following, this entry failed to meet Wiki registration standards and was deleted:

Insufficient direct ties to Nameless Shining Sect.

Overlap with other registered Supernatural Disaster stories.

A ghost tale rejected from the Wiki.

And my job... is to register this with Baekilmong Corporation.

I looked into the bathtub, at the rainwater within.

.......

Is that even allowed?

Chapter 216

A dulled mind stares into the darkness ahead and thinks...

A ghost tale deleted from the Wiki.

And yet, it exists here. That is—

“...Perhaps because it exists even as a deletion record.”

Tales that once met the upvote threshold for Wiki inclusion but were later removed for failing to meet formal criteria could still be found as “Deleted Tales.”

If that’s the case—

Was this ghost tale truly a power created by the Nameless Shining Sect?

Or, since it was deleted for “lack of clear ties to the Nameless Shining Sect,” is it something entirely separate?

...This place that has summoned me, in this grotesque body—

What sort of structure does this world truly have...?

Confusion spreads slowly, riding the slope of a collapsing brain.

The smoke wavers.

“...You okay?”

A voice.

The one in the badger mask.

He’s watching from just outside the bathroom.

It seems he wants to speak.

Standby

But I must decide.

That is—if this tale, potentially belonging to the Nameless Shining Sect, ends up in the hands of Baekilmong Corporation, what effects or side-effects might—

.......

.......

Does it matter?

It doesn’t.

Speculating on the tale’s origins, legitimacy, or contradictions serves no purpose. My sole task is to carry out the assignment.

That is the purpose of my employment contract. Nothing else is needed.

I have one job.

Verification in Progress

To determine whether this darkness is suitable for Dream Residue Production by Baekilmong Corporation.

The three criteria:

Whether dream residue can be collected through “clear.”

Whether survival and return rates can be increased using a manual.

I can infer—

The viable “clear” method is likely enduring until the rain stops, without making any contact with rainwater.

It’s a rain-day hallucination.

Since this ghost tale relies more on luring than direct violence or slaughter, merely keeping one’s mind intact and locking the door increases survival odds.

With enough exploration records, behavioral patterns could be extrapolated.

‘Items 1 and 2—satisfied.’

And the third condition:

Whether it is easily containable or storable.

.......

I looked down at the bathtub, filled with rainwater, and tapped my chin slowly with a fingertip.

Assessing.

And I reached a conclusion.

Conditions Met

: Suitable Darkness

Then—

Proceeding with subjugation for transportable containment form

I moved into action.

“Roe deer? Right now...”

I veiled the bathtub rainwater in mist.

What is now hidden in mist will soon return to its original form.

Unnecessary descriptions and records will be deleted.

Back to its essence.

[Bitmul Meari – Echo of the Rainpit]

On rainy days, it sings to the intended sacrifice who approaches the vicinity of the pit, and in the process, mimics the appearance and voice of those who were previously sacrificed to lure people in.

Not only voices, but letters, notes, or even drawings are used in various forms to distort the meaning of the phrase ‘deliver a message,’ and are thus used very broadly.

It takes the form of a binary-choice torment, forcing the victim into a situation where they must agonize over whose message to trust. Inspired by a double-bind moral torture device, its reality is that whatever is chosen, it leads to a trap, and the sacrifice is captured.

A trap that exploits the human psychology of believing they can survive if they choose the correct one.

This thing—having long since learned how to lure prey—had evolved far beyond the crude superstitions it once imitated.

“It’s learned deception.”

The dead victim becomes a new lure, mixing truth and lies into plausible choices, and uses them to draw in the next sacrifice.

It disguises itself. Mimics.

Mocks from both sides.

You listen to one voice—and start to doubt.

You listen to the other—and doubt again.

By the time that cycle begins, it's already “too late.”

Each time the prey senses wrongness, recognizes a shred of truth, their mind wears down further—until finally, they choose a side and follow its voice.

No matter which they pick, the ending is the same.

It ends here.

Inside this rainwater.

This time, it just happened to be the couple—and the child’s handwriting on the sketchbook.

But even that... is over now.

Wearing Nostalgia Kitty was a mistake.

No effect is ever purely beneficial. You cannot take only the good. You cannot obtain merely the human shape, a body suitable for interaction.

Baekilmong Corporation Internal Database Update

Security Control Team Incident Report

130666 Containment Breach Incident

October 28th, ████, approximately 1:26 AM

Report on the emergency breach where Subject 130666 escaped from its isolation room located in Corridor L-13-6.

Progress Summary

130666 utilized black smoke emitted from its gas mask to disable the lock on the steel door, exited into the containment corridor, proceeded directly to the rental storage unit, then accessed the 17th floor research wing via the main elevator.

Elapsed time: 3 minutes 13 seconds. No security protocol activated.

Disabled: 4 security personnel in containment and storage zones.

Evacuated: 15 researchers on 17th floor.

Disabled: 6 researchers (those who failed to evacuate).

(Those six exhibited strange psychological trauma after inhaling smoke—possible contamination under investigation.)

130666 entered Office of Research Team 1, engaged in direct one-on-one interaction with assigned researcher (Team 1 Lead Kwak Jaegang).

Subject was recovered in its containment cell 6 minutes and 12 seconds later.

Notable Details

CCTV Error: No recorded footage due to simultaneous failure in the isolation room and Research Team 1’s office. Suspected to be caused by 130666’s smoke.

Researcher Survival: Minor limb loss (distal extremity), chest bruising, microfractures on ribs 5 and 6. No life-threatening injuries. Healing potion administered.

Passive Testimony from Assistant: Control officer and assistant present during the event gave weak, unassertive statements about the subject’s danger. Investigation into 130666’s potential for psychic charm/mental manipulation required.

Assistant claimed no control gear was equipped, as incident occurred after business hours.

(Disciplinary action discussed but dismissed due to no rule violation. Work schedules for control staff will be expanded to prevent recurrence.)

Outcome

Security Reinforcement: Enhanced containment protocols. Mandatory additional training via video. Preparation of disciplinary isolation. Increased patrols in L-block corridors.

Research Expansion: Authorization granted to conduct experiments to determine 130666’s abilities.

Several Days Later...

130666 assigned new tasks. Based on experiments, the researcher concluded that the cause of the containment breach was boredom.

Deputy Lee Seonghae lifted her head.

As the office door opened, an employee entered and greeted her with cheerful familiarity, bowing.

“I’m Kang Ihak! Oh, but you can just call me Pony!”

“Yep. Hello~.”

They were acquainted.

If you survive about a year after joining the company, you start remembering more and more coworkers.

There was even an event that served as proof of that.

Promotion to section chief.

“Oh, you just got promoted to section chief?”

“Yes, yes, yes. We all got promoted together. Haha, it’s great not having to treat everyone!”

Kang Ihak chuckled and thumped his chest.

Indeed.

Roughly half the field exploration team rookies who survived without dying or retiring were promoted in bulk to section chief just before this year’s new employee recruitment period.

Of course, a few high performers had been promoted two or three months earlier... but that was a path Kang Ihak had no desire to follow.

He had recently realized:

“No reason to create unnecessary spending!”

If it looks like you’re doing well, people start expecting you to give something. That’s just how shameless people are by nature!

This place didn’t exactly shower early-promoted staff with riches or drop generous darkness rewards into their mouths.

Of course, if a significant bonus was on the table—say, something shiny and golden, or glittering and green—that might be a different story!

But a modest pay bump? From a grand perspective, that was actually a loss.

Thanks to that, he had successfully resisted the dopamine craving for short-term rewards....

“Some people just like being a boss first, I guess.”

Kang Ihak recalled a colleague who had been promoted ahead of him.

The staffer who always wore an eye patch beneath a goat mask—Baek Saheon.

That one had been obsessed with information and items from the start, buttering up higher-ups left and right, and eventually got promoted first.

“He really invested boldly in extracting intel from senior staff early on!”

He’d gone quiet lately for some reason. Likely discovered the eternal truth—that investing in darkness pays better.

And now that his thoughts had reached that far, he recalled other colleagues too.

Those no longer with the HQ’s field exploration team.

“Ah~ There was someone who got promoted real fast, and one who was seriously by-the-book... ah, do you remember them?”

Kim Soleum. Ko Yeongeun.

The two of them exchanged nods, remembering their fellow employees.

“Yeah, they were good people. Such a shame...”

Lee Seonghae furrowed her brow with a look of genuine regret.

Sensing the mood, Kang Ihak smoothly shifted the topic with a pleasant smile. No need to dampen a boss’s mood over something that won’t bring in a single won!

“Congratulations to you too, Deputy~ I heard you got reassigned to Team B along with the promotion!”

“Oh, right~.”

Lee Seonghae nodded.

“Haha, how’s Team B treating you?”

“Mm. Work’s the same wherever you go!”

But intel from the elite team was always valuable.

Kang Ihak sweetened his tone, aiming to curry a bit more favor.

“Oh come on, even if the job’s the same, the people you work with changed. If there’s anything you want to share—just between us...”

“Eh? Me?”

“There’s no need, of course. Yep!”

Don’t poke the boss! Again: there’s no need to sour your boss’s mood!

Especially when the person in question is rumored to have been transferred to Team B after helping dunk two of her Team C superiors into the darkness!

Long-term strategy, long-term strategy...

After a quick tactical adjustment, Kang Ihak sat down politely.

“Then I’ll get right to studying the materials too!”

“Yep, yep.”

Just as they finished their pre-work small talk and were wrapping up reading the documents on the table—

“Greetings!”

A research team staffer arrived.

The person who would be briefing them on today’s operation.

Section Chief Kwak Jaegang of Research Team 1. As he entered from a different department, the two stood up respectfully.

“Have you both gone through the materials?”

“Yes, we have!”

“Wow~ I expected that from our elite Deputy Lee, but Section Chief Kang, you’re pretty fast too. Must be the promotion kicking in!”

“Haha, thank you!”

Nope. It’s because I was looking for a way to squeeze out a side profit from this mission.

And Kwak Jaegang knew that too—but he laughed anyway as he looked between the two, before performing the most important task of the day.

Introducing the shadow holding the power of life and death over him.

“Well then... This is the person you’ll be working under today!”

The office door opened.

And the one who emerged with black smoke—

A figure with massive bone-like horns rising like a crown, eyes glowing like yellow lanterns, and an eerie light shining through the security team’s black uniform.

At the familiar sight, Lee Seonghae’s eyes widened in astonishment.

The owner of the Flower Golden Resort!

“Masco... I mean, Security Team Staffer!”

To think they’d meet again!

Wearing a beaming smile, Lee Seonghae stood up and approached.

“It’s really great to see you again! Have you been well?”

The black-uniformed special division staffer of the security team didn’t so much as flinch and merely looked down at her.

One eye seemed to glance at the researcher momentarily, but then turned toward Lee Seonghae and lowered their head, raising a hand—

Question: Current health condition

“Oh, I’m always in great health!”

Still the same polite and kind employee as ever!

“As expected from our elite Team Dolphin Deputy! You’ve even worked with someone as powerful-looking as this security team staffer!”

Now understanding the situation, Kang Ihak quickly jumped in and seized the chance.

“It’s truly an honor. Please feel free to call me Pony, staffer!”

He even extended a handshake.

“......”

“......”

Ah.

He messed up.

The moment that chilling realization ran through his head, a gloved hand emerged from the black smoke and took the offered hand.

“...!”

To his surprise, the other party accepted the handshake.

But a strange, overwhelming sense of danger climbed up Kang Ihak’s neck.

It was cold.

A shiver ran down his spine.

An instinctual alarm—warning of danger, power, and greatness.

...And yet, there was something vaguely familiar about it?

As if they’d crossed paths before, somewhere deep within the darkness....

“Alright then. This staffer can’t exactly walk into a broadcast station, so we’ve called on you two from the field exploration team.”

But before Kang Ihak could piece it together, the handshake ended and the mission briefing resumed.

Smiling, Kwak Jaegang explained why these two had been summoned.

They needed two people who were familiar with the darkness but not heavily contaminated by it.

“The ones who usually assist this staffer are also security team members—they can’t enter the station during the day. Ha-ha!”

Exactly.

This particular urban legend had surfaced in a broadcast station—a civilian location under public scrutiny—so proper confirmation required cooperation from station personnel.

Supernatural entities that drew too much attention, or security team members who couldn’t go out during the day, weren’t suitable for the task.

When the researcher explained they’d already secured a producer’s cooperation, Lee Seonghae smiled.

“So we’re going to fetch some dangerous darkness from a broadcast station!”

“That’s right!”

“Mm, sounds good!”

If Baekilmong successfully contained this darkness, ordinary people would no longer be at risk from the rumor.

Rarely did the premise begin with something so clearly good, so Lee Seonghae gladly welcomed the assignment.

Soon, the method of transporting the “staffer” was revealed.

“Here, the staffer will stay inside this case. You two will just carry it.”

The staffer was somehow “stored” in a black transport case as if it were the most natural thing in the world—and the two field team members felt that eerie sense of the uncanny they’d come to associate with the darkness.

And as a precaution, they were issued communication devices.

A pair of miniature radios.

“These link directly to security team handlers... Just press the button in case of an emergency.”

The researcher laughed, saying a rookie might be nervous even just from that.

A brief test call was made.

Click.

[Mmm... Can you hear me....]

“Yes, loud and clear!”

“Yep! This is Team I’s Pony Section Chief, and I’m the Dolphin from Team B! Looking forward to working with you!”

Silence.

[...Team B?]

“Yes.”

[...I see.]

[Mm... Carry it carefully... Don’t drop it...]

The languid male voice slowly explained:

How to contact the security team—including himself—should any problems arise.

And even how to communicate simply with the “staffer” inside the transport case.

[If they agree... they’ll knock once. If they don’t... they’ll knock twice.]

“Oh, got it.”

Kang Ihak picked up on it immediately.

That the conversation between Deputy Lee Seonghae and the security team voice was being watched by Section Chief Kwak Jaegang, who was barely suppressing his amused smile.

But that had nothing to do with him!

Realizing there wasn’t a scent of money in that direction, Kang Ihak quickly turned his attention elsewhere.

To where the money was.

To the transport case that held the staffer.

That dark, black, icy object sat alone on the floor, exuding an eerie presence....

“Haha... So, shall I carry it? Seems like the kind of task that should go to the junior, right?”

Just for a second.

If I could somehow lift this transport case and sneak it away somewhere?

There’s supposedly a section in the annex where, if you offer something entertaining, it gets weighed and returned in gold—what if I offered this case?

Kang Ihak approached the transport case....

“......”

The dolphin who had been silently watching spoke up.

“Hey.”

“...Yes?”

“I’ll carry it. The case.”

“...Understood!”

And so, Deputy Lee Seonghae picked up the transport case.

It was cold and light.

There was deep darkness within, yet without hesitation, Lee Seonghae stroked the iron bars and asked:

“You comfy in there?”

Tap.

“That’s a relief!”

She hummed a little tune and hugged the transport case close with care.

Then headed off with Pony toward the site—

The broadcast station.

“Um, excuse me, are you the folks the company introduced? Dreamwalking?”

“Yes, yes.”

They were guided to the variety department editing room through a pre-arranged staff-only entrance.

There, a PD with dark circles under his eyes greeted them alone, looking visibly anxious.

Trusting the sophisticated identity Baekilmong Corporation used when conducting unavoidable supernatural operations among civilians, he took a deep breath and continued speaking.

“To think I’d actually meet people who specialize in this kind of thing... Is it really true you can, like, exorcise something?”

“Of course.”

Perhaps because the two wore suits and masks—looking bizarre enough to inspire confidence—the PD regained a bit of calm.

But his face was still pale. The skin on his face had sunken; he was gaunt with anxiety and fear.

And it wasn’t just the PD. On the way here, everyone they passed—the head writer, the assistant writers, even the lighting director—looked equally unwell.

Clearly, this was something everyone knew about.

“So, uh... you’ve heard the story, right? About the current situation...?”

Deputy Lee Seonghae recalled something.

An internet post that had been attached to the documents on the table.

A spooky urban legend circulating among civilians.

[Has anyone seen this man on PBS shows? (Image Warning)]

You remember PBS, right?

Even kids in school now have probably heard of it.

It was an old broadcasting station from about 20 years ago—long since defunct.

Back in the early 2000s, a lot of trending variety shows aired on PBS. Stuff like “Community Sports Day,” “The Family Appears,” “Hot Talk Talk,” “Happy Saturday Night,” that kind of thing.

There were various images attached.

Big, colorful captions. Low-resolution quality. Exaggerated audience reactions and sound effects.

It all looked a bit tacky now—but oddly nostalgic. Snapshots from variety shows of the 2000s.

And they all had one thing in common.

Lots of people on screen.

You know those variety shows where there’s just a flood of people?

Loads of panelists, or a special episode with dozens of contestants, or at least hundreds of audience members whose reactions get picked up on screen.

Those kinds of shows used to dominate, before observation reality shows or YouTube content took off. Some people still miss them. Others say they were too chaotic and outdated... but anyway, that format basically ruled back then.

And during that era, a certain rumor quietly spread among PDs and editing staff.

Check this out.

(Image)

It was a photo that looked like a screencap from a segment of “Community Sports Day,” taken on a traditional paper-mat set.

A show just described from PBS.

But the photos didn’t stop there.

They continued—seemingly random screencaps from various other shows.

“The Family Appears,” “Hot Talk Talk,” “Happy Saturday Night”... and finally—

Notice anything?

The same images were shown again, this time with red circles highlighting something.

One person.

It’s the same man every time.

A man with dark eyes staring into the camera.

Wearing a cap. Smiling faintly without showing his teeth.

Apparently, in most PBS variety shows, this guy was spotted among the cast, the audience, or just somewhere in the crowd.

One of the editors noticed something weird, looked into it, and yep—he was really there.

And as people kept talking about it...

They realized something else.

This man only ever shows up...

In episode six.

A caption followed the last photo.

To be exact, there’s a rumor he only appears in episodes where the number ends in 6.

Creepy, right?

Well, there are lots of urban legends in the TV industry. Ghost sightings, strange noises... stuff like that.

So back then, staff just gossiped about it, and it even made it into some horror zines. Maybe a few viewers caught on too.

Honestly, though, it could just be coincidence. It’s not impossible for the same guy to keep popping up as a panelist or audience member.

But then something changed.

A newspaper clipping was attached.

It was about a power outage at the broadcast station that wiped editing data.

Due to the blackout, saved editing footage was lost. This affected both documentary and news departments... but here’s the important part.

Apparently, the final cut of “Hot Talk Talk” Episode 166 was among the affected files.

In the end, Episode 166 aired as a highlight compilation from previous episodes.

And—

That’s when things started getting truly strange.

Because...

In every single highlight clip—

That man was visible.

Chapter 220

The tone of the post suddenly plummets into chilling descent.

Due to the power outage, the episode was hastily compiled and aired.

Episode 166.

A patchwork of segments from earlier broadcasts—each scene capturing panelists and audience members, included in the post as photos.

And...

There’s no need to circle the man’s face anymore.

Because anyone who’s read this far now remembers it.

Those black eyes.

That smiling mouth.

The man on screen is the only one looking directly into the camera amid dozens of people, across scenes from cheerful, noisy variety shows of the past.

Episodes 157, 158, 160, 162, 164...

Every episode used to compile 166.

There, in some corner of the boisterous show...

Back then, this man had never been spotted in those original episodes.

If he had been, the whole theory about him only appearing in episode 6 wouldn’t exist.

But now—when they were re-edited into 166—he was there.

He hadn’t been there before. No one had noticed him.

But now... now he was.

And here. Compare these two.

Two images side-by-side.

One was a zoomed-in capture from earlier, the “man on screen” marked with a red circle.

The other, a close-up from episode 166.

Same angle. Same frame. And when you compare them—

His expression changed.

Only in 166 does the man smile wide, baring all his teeth.

And the black pupils staring at the camera seem... strangely dilated.

A creeping chill oozes from the low-resolution image.

In every highlight clip, he wore that expression.

An ice-cold dread slithers down the spine.

Realizing this was no ordinary incident, the station’s internal staff took action among themselves.

They began inspecting people more carefully, whether at indoor sets or outdoor locations.

Panelists, audience members—everyone.

Trying to catch anyone who looked like “the man on screen.”

It was the 2000s. Back then, identity checks weren’t nearly as strict.

So this sudden increase in scrutiny stirred up gossip among celebrities and on-site crew.

That’s when the screencaps of “the man” started circulating.

And the theories piled up—

That he was a PD who died in a broadcast accident.

That he was the former director’s son, who committed suicide.

That he was a construction worker who died during station renovations.

That an occult horror special a few years prior had summoned something.

But none of it was ever proven.

Rituals, exorcisms—nothing made the phenomenon stop.

He just kept being spotted...

So eventually, the staff changed their approach.

What did they do after that?

They just kept cutting him out.

Whenever a final edit was ready, they’d review the entire thing frame by frame in the editing room—

and if that man showed up, they’d slice the shot.

That’s how they made sure he stopped appearing in aired episodes.

The urban legend faded with time.

After all, if no one saw the man again, people lost interest.

And whenever someone did claim to spot him again, it turned out to be just someone with a similar look—

and that actually helped debunk the story.

That’s how the whole thing faded out.

That’s what I thought, up until recently...

But then I heard something bone-chilling from a screenwriter friend.

It’s too long, so I’ll share it in the next post.

And then—

“...He’s started appearing again.”

Finishing her brief mental replay of “Document 1,” Deputy Lee Seonghae stood face to face with the PD, who had barely managed to swallow his saliva, speaking with a pale face.

Terror carved into every line of his expression.

“You mean... ‘the man on screen’?”

“Yes......”

The strange, terrifying phenomenon that once plagued a now-defunct broadcaster was now manifesting again—right here, in the MBS channel’s variety department.

“We’ve been trying to delete or avoid him as best we can... But this doesn’t seem like a normal case. So we all agreed to bring in professionals.”

The two field exploration team staffers, now promoted to makeshift exorcists, remained calm and composed as always.

They didn’t care what they were called.

“Whatever ritual or rite you perform—just please, make sure he stops showing up. Ideally, in a peaceful way if possible...”

“Of course! No need to worry!”

Once it’s retrieved by the company, it’ll no longer appear in this station.

Lee Seonghae smiled quietly as she recalled the proposed countermeasure for “the man on screen.”

The anomaly is currently estimated to be Grade C–D.

Displays classic symptoms of supernatural visual interference in video media.

If unobserved: Appears more frequently.

If observed: Appears more violently.

– Maintain the anomaly’s irregular emergence and aggression level until optimal moment,

then store the video data in a non-playable format and transfer it to secure storage. (Estimated)

Failure may result in death.

The countermeasure detailed in the report was surprisingly thorough.

Almost like a cleared walkthrough—though it came with repeated disclaimers about lack of verified field data.

“If all goes well, maybe the staffer won’t have to do anything!”

Better to save them for the final move.

“Well then, I’ll guide you from here.”

“Yes, thank you!”

The PD led them toward the editing room.

No Entry

Under Construction

The door creaked open.

Screeeeeak.

Contrary to the posted sign, the room was dark and completely intact, with not a soul inside.

All the monitors were off, the equipment powered down.

“The sign’s just to keep others from coming in. For now, we only handle urgent edits that require in-house equipment during the day—the rest we do offsite, in studio or remotely.”

A modern solution that wasn’t available back in the defunct station days.

With editing software and hardware now broadly accessible and standardized, such workarounds were finally feasible.

But inside, the editing room felt... cold and stagnant, devoid of life.

“Hm...”

As they stepped in, Kang Ihak’s mouth opened again.

“Yeah... this isn’t just your average haunting.”

“...!”

Clicking his tongue, Kang Ihak grew serious.

“Yeah, this feels dangerous.”

“.......”

“In cases like this, we usually need more prep time, and a longer schedule.”

“Uh... How long would that take?”

Kang Ihak looked the PD in the eye and solemnly declared:

“Congratulations.”

“...Excuse me?”

“You’re lucky today. Right now, you’re standing next to the most spiritual object we could possibly bring.”

Tap.

He gestured toward the transport case and gave it a light knock.

“That means we can skip prep time altogether. In fact, this isn’t just an extra charge kind of situation—we’d usually have to draft a whole new estimate.”

“Oh...”

Kang Ihak looked at him with reassuring confidence.

“But this time, we’ll just do it for you.”

“...!”

For the sake of these hardworking TV folks. For the people bringing joy to the nation. Even if it meant they’d take the loss... they’d shoulder it just this once.

The PD—his mental state already battered by fear and exhaustion—blankly nodded at Kang Ihak’s dazzling rhetoric.

“Th-thank you...”

“Haha, keep making great shows. Just one thing, though.”

Kang Ihak’s expression turned stern.

“We’ll need three days here in the editing room. No one comes in. Got it?”

“Yes!”

“And of course, for a case like this, the extended schedule... will naturally come with a cost. Nothing we can do about that.”

With flair, she delivered the follow-up: “As you can see, we can’t really invoice officially for this kind of work, so we’ll need payment in cash or goods only.”

And so, the PD ran down to the first floor of the station to withdraw money—then even took off his own gold accessories and handed them over.

Kang Ihak accepted them all without hesitation, bowing politely.

“You’ll sleep peacefully tonight!”

“Thank you...”

And as the PD disappeared from the editing room, Kang Ihak turned to the group, arms full of “extra profit,” grinning from ear to ear.

“We just tell them we managed to do a three-day job in a single night thanks to our miraculous skills!”

Unbelievable finesse.

“Whoa.”

Watching quietly, Lee Seonghae finally spoke.

“You’re... really good at deceiving people, huh?”

“Deceiving? No no—this is creating side income in ways that hurt no one and bother no one!”

Kang Ihak quickly split the gold and cash into three equal portions.

His hands moved smoothly, as if by instinct.

“Besides, that guy saved money on future exorcisms. And if not, he’ll just file a report to the higher-ups or somewhere else to get reimbursed, right?”

“.......”

“So come on, let’s split this by headcount. One for me, one for Deputy Dolphin here... and one for our stylish staffer!”

Kang Ihak spun toward the transport case and offered a portion of the gold.

“Staffer?”

“Yes, of course! I mean, if cash isn’t ideal, then gold will do just fine. What do you think, staffer?”

As if they would accept it—

Tap.

“......”

“See? Told you so!”

Sure enough—it had to be a being who understood the greatness of gold. Kang Ihak silently pumped a victory fist into the air.

Lee Seonghae froze briefly, then looked down at the transport case and asked seriously:

“Um... Do you need money?”

Tap.

“Should I give you more?”

Tap.

“Excuse me, does the company not pay you a salary?”

Silence.

The transport case remained quiet. Somehow, it felt like the mascot staffer was confused. Better not to push too hard.

But... maybe they really don’t get paid?

As Lee Seonghae mulled this over, she decided to ease the mind of this kind staffer.

With work talk.

“I’ll do my best today!”

Tap-tap.

Does that mean it’s okay if she doesn’t? As expected, what a kind person!

“Well then... shall we begin the operation now?”

“Yes.”

With that brief episode of capitalism over, Kang Ihak swiftly moved to the entrance of the editing room and shut the door.

Thud.

The editing room was now sealed.

“...Turning on the monitor.”

The central console lit up, and a variety show file appeared on the desktop.

And without prompting, another memory surfaced.

The reason why those associated with the “Man on Screen” legend were so terrified of the editing room.

Why they were here.

[Has anyone seen this man on PBS shows? (Image Warning) - Part 2]

Hey. Everyone was so curious I brought the second part quickly.

In the last post, I said the “man on screen” story faded because he was all edited out, right?

And that my friend, a broadcast writer, told me something chilling?

Well—

Turns out that was a lie.

They said it was possible at first. That they could remove the man and make clean final cuts.

But over time, as the episode count grew, as the edits piled up... they kept finding him again and again, even after cutting him out.

And his appearance started to change.

Before, he could be brushed off unless you were really paying attention in a crowd. But then, eventually, even just a passing glance made people stop and hit the pause button.

I don’t know exactly what he looked like. The editing staff wouldn’t say. They just kept repeating one thing:

“He looks angry.”

Eventually, they hit their limit.

No matter how many times they deleted him, he kept showing up. The already sleep-deprived editing crew now had to spend nights just hunting for the “man on screen” and trimming him out.

So they came up with a new strategy.

Total prevention.

Just don’t include large crowd shots where “the man” might appear.

No audience footage at all—just dubbed-in reactions. Or tight close-ups on individual panelists.

That way, there’s less chance for too many people to appear on screen at once.

Seemed like it would help. Sounded reasonable.

After that idea was proposed, the atmosphere calmed. Like they’d found a real solution.

The PD and assistant director who had suggested it sighed with relief and went into the editing room to try it.

And the next day—

Both of them were found dead in the editing room.

“......”

The PD had hung himself from the ceiling with a wire.

The AD had sliced open his artery with a shard of a broken monitor, slumped in a chair.

There was a note by the door.

But the handwriting was completely illegible. No one could tell what it was written with.

My writer friend and the PD who cleaned up the scene had worked together before. According to him, the letter looked like it was written by a schizophrenic—rambling, disjointed, just incoherent words scrawled chaotically.

He barely remembered the content, it was so disturbing.

But one night, while recalling the words and trying to piece them together—

He began to understand what it said:

The man on screen doesn’t want to be deleted.

He is angry.

Air him immediately.

I’ve delivered his message.

I don’t want to die.

I’m sorry.

He said he couldn’t sleep that night.

He ended up staying at a temple for a few days.

Even without that, most of the PBS variety staff were already so shaken or terrified that the whole department basically shut down.

People around my age might remember it:

Articles suddenly reporting two deaths in the PBS variety team.

Two to three weeks of back-to-back canceled variety programs, saying they were in mourning...

Yeah. This was what that was about.

They rebuilt the editing room afterward, but the vibe never recovered.

PDs started quitting en masse. Even the screenwriters didn’t want to renew their contracts.

So those who worked at PBS back then all know:

The official explanation for PBS’s closure involves political and financial pressure.

But in truth, the death spiral of the variety department played a huge role.

Still... there is one piece of good news.

PBS shut down in 2008.

And with that, the “man on screen” legend truly ended.

...

...

...

But you know what?

This came from a show that aired yesterday.

At the end of the post was a high-resolution screenshot.

A frame from episode 36 of A Graceful Midnight Burst of Laughter, at 6 minutes and 23 seconds.

An MC and solo guest, chatting happily. The camera briefly cuts to the production crew for a reaction shot.

Sitting among the blurred faces—

Do you recognize him?

The man on screen is staring back.

His mouth split wide into a toothy grin.

Pupils dilated.

Eyebrows twisted in a grotesque expression of rage.

He’s looking at you.

Right through the screen.

PBS is gone.

But all of its equipment and staff were absorbed wholesale and rebranded into a new broadcaster.

Know what it is?

MBS.

And A Graceful Midnight Burst of Laughter is an MBS variety show.

......

Don’t watch MBS variety shows live for a while.

On the monitor in the editing room—

Something began to appear.

Because who knows what might happen.

Chapter 221

A dark, cramped editing room.

Rows of enormous editing monitors.

One of them flickered on.

The only light source revealed a blue desktop background.

And there—several video icons.

They were variety show recordings.

The two field exploration team staffers, having already discussed the plan in advance, moved without hesitation.

“First, we’ll check if it’s really darkness.”

“Yes!”

Verification procedure.

A hand moved the mouse. The pointer slid across the desktop...

Click.

The selected icon opened a video file.

It was the raw footage for what would become Episode 26 of MBS’s Friendly Karaoke Room.

[Hahahaha!]

[Wow, you sing really well!]

[You flatter me.]

On a bright, colorful karaoke set, two guests and an MC laughed across a table.

A trendy talk-show format aimed at YouTube virality: luxurious guests, a non-aggressive, humorous host, and a light, casual mood.

The cheerful set design, laughter, and staff reactions gave it a refreshing vibe.

“There.”

The video paused.

A passing cut of the karaoke set interior.

A small, dark window on the karaoke room door.

There—was a man’s face.

Staring directly into the camera.

“...Phew.”

It could be seen even in the darkness. More distorted and more striking than the capture from Episode 36 in the article.

As if he wanted someone beyond the camera to notice him.

If someone stumbled on this unprepared, it would be horrifying enough to freeze them in place or send them screaming.

“Okay.”

But the two field team staffers quickly closed the video and clicked on another.

They were used to this.

Click, click—hands moved rapidly across the screen, opening episode files that ended in “6” from various variety shows.

But then, realization set in.

“......”

“......”

Modern variety formats rarely feature large groups anymore.

Especially shows using lots of audience participation or mass reaction shots—practically nonexistent.

Which meant—there were no crowds.

Which meant—“the man on screen” could no longer blend in by pretending to be one of many.

And so—

The man began to appear in increasingly unnatural ways.

In a rooftop show where guests sat on floor cushions chatting, he stood outside the veranda, staring into the camera.

In a fishing variety, while the cast focused on their rods by the river, his face leaned in beside them, eyes fixed on the lens.

In an actor interview show, where the MC and guest sat across from each other at a table—he stood motionless in the background, staring straight at the camera.

His expressions became increasingly grotesque.

His postures too.

At times, he even mimicked the guests or MC.

Almost like he was trying to imitate them—but the mimicry was wrong. The uncanny wrongness raised goosebumps up the spine.

“......”

At this point, even a gold-loving opportunist began to feel an ominous dread creeping in.

Kang Ihak swallowed hard and closed the video.

But he didn’t hesitate.

“I’m inserting it.”

“Yes.”

Deputy Lee Seonghae inserted the prepared USB into the editing rig connected to the monitor.

Time to execute the company-issued countermeasure.

Store video data in a non-playable format and send it to an appropriate storage site. (Estimated.)

Based on the darkness’s behavioral pattern, this was a rational attempt.

If people in the editing room kept dying while watching videos featuring the “man on screen,” the solution was to extract and isolate all such files—and then shut down the room itself.

If the videos can’t be played, the phenomenon can’t occur.

Unfortunately, the production staff would have to work from raw footage not yet viewed inside the room.

“So no need to provoke anything—just transfer it directly to the USB, right?”

“Yep.”

Click.

The moment the USB connected, Kang Ihak opened the removable disk and swiftly dragged over every episode file ending in “6,” plus the in-progress edit folder, dumping them all in one go.

He’d done this hundreds of times. Muscle memory.

Phew.

As expected for such large files, a loading popup appeared. Transferring the video files to the external drive. Just a little longer...

Copying...

“......”

“......”

Wait.

“Um, excuse me.”

Lee Seonghae broke the silence.

“Why’s your hand there?”

Kang Ihak looked down at his hand reflexively.

It was resting on the keyboard.

Specifically—pressing the Ctrl key.

...A habit.

When you drag and drop a file or folder onto a USB drive, it defaults to “move.”

That is—cut.

But if you hold Ctrl while dragging—

It performs a copy.

A natural instinct for anyone who's ever juggled external backups during college projects or freelance work.

A backup.

“......”

Kang Ihak stared at the files still transferring into the USB.

And at the originals still sitting untouched on the desktop.

The videos containing “the man on screen.”

At this rate, the raw files would remain in the editing room.

But files in the middle of copying cannot be deleted.

No.

With the blood draining from his face, Kang Ihak stared at the "Copying..." popup and waited.

The numbers rose rapidly. Please. 90, 92, 95, 97, 99...

Complete.

At that moment, Kang Ihak slammed the delete shortcut.

Do you want to permanently delete these 6 items?

Yes!

It had to be now. No time to fumble. He didn’t fumble, but something strange—something supernatural—intervened.

The mouse double-clicked.

Instead of deleting...

He played the file.

You made eye contact with the man on screen.

In the cheerful scene of a variety show,

quietly, alone, from behind the camera—

eyes that have met yours appear.

Dilated pupils, like holes carved into the face—

two dark, black eyes are staring at you.

He bares his teeth

and smiles.

He sees you.

As he opens his mouth,

inside it, black voids

and shredded gums and tongue writhe,

frozen in place.

A mouth that slowly informs you—

Don’t delete it.

AaAAaeeiiIiIeEeaaaEEEiiiaaaeeiiieeKKeiiieeeaAaaaeeeiiiiiIIIEEE—

From the desktop—

all the video files begin playing at once.

Audio overlaps, glitches, stutters.

A garbled, guttural sound.

Something is coming

out.

AaAAaeiiiIEeEEEGgggrrhhEEEAaeeeEE—

The Man on Screen

Larger.

Larger.

The person standing in front of the editing monitor has no choice but to watch.

Kang Ihak’s gaze was fixed.

Lee Seong-hae’s gaze was fixed.

They had no choice but to look.

They were ready—ready to listen to what he had to say.

He was angry.

You listen to everything he says—and then you die.

And obscured the Field Investigation Team’s vision.

Only the lanterns remained in the smoke.

“...Ha!”

At that moment, one of the Field Team agents separated from the darkness sucked in a breath and lifted her body.

...Kang Ihak realized she had leaned in so close to the editing monitor that her nose was practically touching the screen.

A chill prickled across her back.

The audio from all six pieces of footage echoed through the small editing room, distorted like a constant scream.

Kang Ihak could still see it—the thick black fog spilling from the transport crate, and the yellow eyes... Deputy Lee Seong-hae looked almost completely engulfed by the smoke.

“Staff member!”

Kang Ihak made a reasonable request.

“If you could maybe... seal up the monitor tight and just subdue that thing—just once—we’ll continue right away!”

That’s what the Security Team was for, wasn’t it?

But the black smoke didn’t touch the monitor.

...Huh?

Concerns upon contact:

▶ Deletion of darkness

▶ Increased mortality risk for the two accompanying staff (Dolphin, Pony)

“......!!”

Reason: Non-physical entity anomaly. Insufficient data.

Wait.

So even just making contact with it might “influence” that editing room ghost—enough to trigger unpredictable variables... so it was holding back?

And one of those variables was... the disappearance of darkness?

Just how strong is this thing?

A sharp thrill ran down her spine, but her voice came out calm and rational.

“Then to resume the operation...”

And what the Staff member instructed them to do was extremely simple.

Do not gaze into the darkness

“...Of course! A perfectly reasonable point.”

But humans become drastically less accurate and efficient without the use of their eyes...

Especially when they’re being bombarded with insane pop-ups from corrupted footage, and need to locate specific files on the desktop and delete them permanently. Precision work.

“Still... I think someone needs to be watching.”

And the Staff member kindly replied to that too.

Observer: Me

At that moment—

The dozens of yellow eyes rising from the black fog turned and stared directly at the monitor.

...A black background, and yellow eyes.

...Wait?

If delayed:

▶ Failure to collect darkness

▶ Casualties

That was the signal.

Thunk.

The Elite Team agent slipped into the black smoke like a slide, positioning herself beneath the desk with the monitor.

“...!”

Exactly.

Even with the mouse under the desk, it still works just fine...!

Thanks to the strong Bluetooth connections most equipment used nowadays, this was possible.

Lee Seong-hae balanced the mouse on the ground.

...That’ll definitely land it in the bottom-right.

She was aligning the position of the mouse pointer on the invisible screen.

Once that was set, just a small upward nudge... would bring the pointer to the bottom corner of the screen—where the “Show Desktop” button was located.

She clicked.

Input Devices:

▶ Mouse (Dolphin)

▶ Keyboard (Pony)

Damn it.

Guess you work as much as you get paid!

Thus began the mad cycle.

IIIIEEEAAARRRGGGHHHUUUUOOOZZZAAAHHHIIIIIEEEEEEIIIIEEE

You couldn’t just select all the icons on the desktop with the keyboard and delete them. The Recycle Bin would be included—and that couldn’t be deleted.

So she had to manually select the appropriate files with the mouse, excluding the Recycle Bin.

Then Kang Ihak would press the “Permanent Delete” button.

They repeated the process.

Waiting for the exact timing.

Before it was too late.

DEEEELEEEETTEEEEAAAAGGHHHHHHHHZZZZZIIIIIIIEEEAAHHHHHH

Would you like to permanently delete these 6 items?

Thunk.

Yes.

Lee Seong-hae immediately placed her hand on the keyboard Kang Ihak was holding and pressed Enter repeatedly.

To make sure the footage wouldn’t accidentally double-click and start playing again.

Crack.

The USB now contained the “Man Inside the Screen” phenomenon.

They’d survived—and earned their pay.

Dopamine surged madly through her nervous system, making her heart pound. This is what happiness feels like...

“Ah—sorry! I left the original file on this computer halfway through, so we lost some time because of me!”

This kind of thing had to be stated clearly—acknowledging mistakes helped smooth things over with minimal consequences and avoided bigger losses or unexpected expenses later.

Still, I recovered the situation well.

She was still fidgeting with the small magnet held in her left hand.

A personal tool she’d crafted using a free upgrade reward when she was promoted to supervisor. Its function: “Once a day, there’s a 50% chance it’ll instantly unlock something.”

It had the drawback of being ineffective against locks she wouldn’t be able to open with time and effort anyway—but overall, it was a satisfying tool.

And it had done its job this time, too!

If I hadn’t gotten that transport crate open right away, I’d be dead, huh?

Whew! High reward really does come with high risk!

“And for it to end this quickly, too. Must be thanks to working with such capable people... I even got some bonus earnings—really glad it wrapped up nicely!”

The “Staff member,” who had returned to silence inside the transport crate, responded.

Rejection.

He reacted to the act of being deleted.

So if the trigger wasn’t the editing room, but rather the act of editing the footage itself—

“Editing... urgent stuff that needs equipment gets done here during the day, but we try to do most of the work outside the station—either in studios or from home.”

The “Man Inside the Screen”

now existed

–It means... if no more Dream Extract can be pulled out even when someone clears it... then we discard it....

The oddly specific company manual.

A ghost story that disappeared in the 2000s.

And now, this resurgence.

When Darkness loses its supernatural presence, it can no longer yield Dream Extract.

In that case, it is struck from the registry and disposed of. Some are recycled through research or recharge procedures.

One keyword stands out:

Recycled.

“So this was... intentionally released back into the public, wasn’t it? So it could regain its supernatural presence... and yield Dream Extract again...”

The team captain likely already knew.

He must have been working in field investigation when this ghost story was previously registered.

And now, reading this through that lens, even the redacted line in today’s assignment sheet makes sense...

For further information, see ■■Record #C16548.

For further information, see Disposal Record #C16548.

Baekilmong Corporation intentionally re-released this disposed ghost story to the broadcasting station.

To revive it, so it could once again generate Dream Extract.

“......”

–You’re well-informed.

“...!”

–We simply returned a ghost story that was already on location, after managing it properly for a while.

–No one’s losing anything. Of course, since it’s product manufacturing know-how, it mustn’t leave the company, so everyone who heard this must keep their mouths shut.

Lee Seong-hae’s breath is audibly shallow.

Director Cheong’s calm voice continues.

–That’s all, then.

“...Huh?”

–I mean it’s time to return.

“Is the security team coming out?”

–Of course not.

What?

–Why should my company waste valuable personnel and resources on a civilian commotion?

Director Cheong speaks coolly.

–There’s no need to use a tiger-hunting blade to catch a chicken. It’s cumbersome, costly... I loathe inefficiency.

–The cleanup will be handled by the agency that handles cleanups.

...Agency?

–Yes. That’s what those taxpayer-funded institutions are for. You pay taxes, you use them.

–Letting your emotions sway you into blabbing your personal opinions around the company is the kind of thing only fledglings like Ho Youwon do.

Director Ho.

The one who harbors resentment against the agency.

–It’s a win-win, really. That clueless agency will resolve the supernatural disaster, unaware that our company is the only one left with the original ghost story.

...

–You’d best hurry back.

I lift my head.

–It’s been a while since the Disaster Management Bureau was alerted... they should be arriving soon.

From somewhere—

A bell begins to ring.

Chapter 223

The sound of a bell.

Tinkle.

A clear tone—proof of existence.

A sign of some sacred force that repels the profane.

Tinkle.

Next comes the sound of footsteps.

Brisk and unhesitating, a warning-paced stride that heads straight for its destination.

Lively, yet not frivolous.

The footsteps grow nearer and nearer to the officetel, pausing briefly at the open door where a shadow is cast.

And then...

“Let’s see. The civilian who got here first is... oh, no.”

An agent of the Disaster Management Bureau reveals himself.

A long scar crossing his throat.

A smile.

Hyeonmu Team 1.

Once my superior.

“A cult company, I see.”

Agent Choi.

His smiling mouth moves, but the eyes scanning the blood-soaked living room and the corpse that gouged out its own eyes are devoid of laughter.

“This wasn’t a coincidence, was it?”

“...!”

Tinkle.

Agent Choi gestures behind him. But we already know what’s there.

A massive guillotine.

An illusory glow of bluish light shimmers faintly from the blade.

Agent Choi moves his arm...

“Well, then—”

“We surrender!”

Kang Ihak raises both hands and slams to her knees dramatically.

“...?!”

[Oh my, seems your knees go on sale quickly!]

But Agent Choi doesn’t even blink.

“Yeah? Even better.”

“Wait! Wait a sec!”

Kang Ihak speaks faster than the guillotine swings.

“C’mon, Agent! You remember me, right? We met during that one Darkness—the expensive one with the conch shell!”

“Sure I remember.”

Agent Choi smiles thinly.

“You were the one from the cult company who tried to use injured kids as hostages to escape, weren’t you?”

“That wasn’t me! That was Assistant Manager Nabi acting on her own!”

The Security Team Captain watches the banter with a blank look, as if wondering what the hell this is.

“We were just scouting for viable Darkness, but saw there was no way we were getting involved and were just about to leave!”

“Ooh~ So you’re totally unrelated to all this?”

“That’s not the point. The point is—”

Kang Ihak quickly turns to the Security Captain and solemnly asks,

“Is Director Cheong still on the line?”

“She’s... already disconnected.”

“Okay.”

Kang Ihak flashes a wide smile and turns back to Agent Choi.

“We can sell you the info we gathered on this Darkness—at the lowest price!”

She makes a small tight circle with her thumb and forefinger for emphasis.

[Good grief.]

“C’mon now, lowest price? A decent public servant should pay full price for goods, don’t you think, Citizen?”

Agent Choi smiles as he pulls his hand out from behind his back.

One step forward.

“I’ll make sure your prison canteen meals are well-stocked.”

The guillotine flies.

THUD.

But a beastlike black forepaw blocks it.

“...!”

The Security Captain’s mutated left arm.

Yet even so, the blade digs deep into it, nearly slicing it clean off between the monstrous claws.

Ssssssshhh.

The point of contact hisses and burns.

Even just looking at that trembling limb is enough to imagine overwhelming pain, but the Security Captain doesn’t flinch.

He simply stares at Agent Choi over the edge of the blade.

“Look... we’re just gonna head back now, alright? We’re not gonna pull anything... we’ll cooperate... I swear...”

“Come on now. How do I extract cooperation if I let you leave—”

Agent Choi’s chuckling answer is cut off.

His expression changes.

He looks at the Security Captain again.

“...You.”

It’s as if the transformation helped him realize what the uniform had concealed—recognition dawning at last.

The night Director Ho placed the contract constraints on me.

Back in the dream incubation chamber, when I was exposed as a spy by Agent Choi—

He had met the Security Captain there.

“......”

A powerful surge of conflict and emotion briefly clouds Agent Choi’s face.

But the feeling is quickly repressed.

His tone shifts back to that dry, bureaucratic politeness.

“...Security Team, huh? This must be serious. I’ll need full testimony. ASAP.”

“This is all a misunderstanding. We were just hoping to make a little side profit—if you can just guarantee that part...”

“We’ll tell you everything.”

“...!”

Lee Seong-hae steps forward, raising her right hand.

“D-Deputy?!”

“Yeah?”

“Yes, sir.”

Lee Seong-hae doesn’t even glance at Kang Ihak as she smiles brightly.

“We should help good-hearted agents like you working to save citizens.”

“......”

“So could you please put the guillotine away? The Security Team staff member looks like he’s in a lot of pain!”

Agent Choi studies Lee Seong-hae’s face, then finally retracts the guillotine and smiles, habitually.

“Shall we, then?”

He quickly listens to Lee Seong-hae’s briefing.

She explains everything—what the ‘Man in the Screen’ did, how it’s certain to spiral out of control—without skipping a detail.

I worried she might even mention the USB in her hand, but after glancing back and forth between the transport case (containing me and the Security Captain), she omits it.

Likely thinking it might bring us harm.

“Okay.”

...And throughout it all, the guillotine blade remained pressed against the front of the transport case.

I held my breath.

To keep the smoke from leaking out.

So they’d have no clue.

Agent Choi listens to Lee Seong-hae’s testimony without relaxing for a second.

Only at the very end does he retract the guillotine.

“Whew. Thank you for your cooperation.”

“Oh, not at all! Helping good people is the right thing to do!”

“Wow~ For real? That’s what I do every day, you know. Fulfillment through work—helping decent citizens. But...”

Agent Choi peers at the golden mark on Lee Seong-hae’s mask.

An elite agent of the Dolphin Team.

“Why’d you join Baekilmong?”

“......”

“You do know that plenty of good people have died because of that company’s work, right?”

Lee Seong-hae smiles brightly.

“Well, I try to stop any bad stuff I can see from happening!”

“Do you? Why not just quit?”

“......”

She doesn’t answer. And Agent Choi lets it drop there.

[Oh, looks like it’s almost time to leave.]

Indeed.

This was a simultaneous event affecting multiple editors. The agents were likely dispatched in tandem to cover different locations.

Having gathered what he needed here, Agent Choi will probably leave the cleanup to the Fabrication Team and head out to support another Hyeonmu unit...

I know this.

I used to do it too.

[...Friend, you seem to be in low spirits. Oh dear, are you alright?]

I’m fine.

Or rather—maybe I’m not. But it doesn’t matter.

This sluggish brain can’t come to any conclusions anyway.

I don’t even know what I’m feeling.

I simply watch, through the bars of the transport case, as a familiar silhouette steps out through the officetel’s front door—

“Hey, you.”

Just before closing the door, Agent Choi turns back and approaches, lowering his voice.

He speaks to the Security Captain.

“You know who I am, right?”

“......”

“And you know that employee who goes by the nickname ‘Noru,’ too.”

The Security Captain meets Agent Choi’s gaze.

“What happened to him? Don’t bother saying he was declared dead after going missing during the Train ghost story. That means nothing. I want to know what happened after.”

“......”

“Did he really... get a Wish Permit?”

The Security Captain answers slowly.

“He... got it.”

“......”

Agent Choi’s face goes blank.

“And?”

“......”

“And then what?”

The Security Captain gently taps my transport case, then begins to turn his head toward me—

CLANG.

The guillotine blade slams into the cage.

“Use of supernatural disaster is prohibited.”

A flash of familiar light bursts beyond the bars.

“Come on now, sir. Showing up to the scene with a suspicious mobile case like that—anyone would think it’s portable isolation for a supernatural threat.”

“......”

“Shall I split it open and see what it’s for?”

“It’s... not like that.”

“Then answer me.”

“......”

The Security Captain didn’t answer.

Because he hadn’t confirmed my intent.

And as the silence dragged on for a few seconds, Agent Choi—who should have already been moving to assist with civilian rescue amid an active supernatural disaster—showed signs of impatience... then gave up.

Gave up on getting an answer here and now.

“Right. Well... I guess you might not feel like talking. That’s your right.”

Instead, he chose another path.

“But, you know, maybe after a hot bowl of seolleongtang and some rest, you’ll feel like opening up.”

The moment Agent Choi reached into his coat with a grin, I realized it.

“Besides, this whole situation is suspicious. Really suspicious.”

“......!”

“A cult company just happening to show up chasing Darkness after hearing rumors... and the timing of the report? Way too convenient. Don’t you think?”

Uncannily accurate intuition.

“People involved in disaster scenes often recall more after some time and reflection. That’s how witness statements work.”

And from his coat came—

A mobile glass containment unit.

“We’ll provide a place for you to rest while you wait. Courtesy of the Bureau.”

The agent had decided to transport all Baekilmong Corporation personnel at once—just in case.

...No.

I need to finish the mission and return with the USB.

I dispersed my smoke.

Just as I was about to slip out of the transport cage—

“Hey.”

A cold voice echoed through the officetel.

Someone new had entered.

She grabbed Agent Choi’s arm.

“Cool your head.”

“...!”

A tall woman wearing the Sword of Divine Calamity at her side.

Team Leader Haegŭm of Hyeonmu Team 3.

The same agent I encountered at the Yukhoe Theme Park.

[Ah, indeed. The employee who assisted you in running the resort, yes!]

Exactly.

“When dealing with cult companies, if a Team Leader’s present, they’re the one who takes over. Rookie agents should go tend to the TV station.”

“......”

“Still so full of fire, trying to use shortcuts and loopholes.”

“Aw, come on, noona. I’m basically in the prime of youth!”

Agent Choi smiled, but his tone carried urgency.

“But I still have something I need to—”

“I said go. Agent.”

“......”

“The real chaos has already been contained. Now it’s time to go butter up the station director. That’s your job.”

Agent Choi stood rooted for a moment, then finally turned and walked away.

The sound of his footsteps faded as he ran off.

......

“Whew.”

Team Leader Haegŭm straightened up and looked over the two masked figures.

“Alright. If the on-site investigation team has nothing more to say, you can be on your way.”

“...!”

“Yes, thank you very much!”

“Wait. Can the Security Team staff leave too?”

“Ah, I’ve got business with them. Not sending them to jail or anything, so don’t worry.”

“Then we’ll just wait—”

“Yessir! Thank you!”

Kang Ihak bolted for the door, and after a moment of hesitation, Lee Seong-hae followed. The field investigation team exited the officetel.

Team Leader Haegŭm watched their retreating backs with a slightly bitter expression, then approached the Security Captain.

And then...

She looked down.

At me.

“Golden Mascot.”

...!!

“Or should I call you the resort owner?”

Team Leader Haegŭm smiled wryly.

“Why so surprised? You hired me as a resort employee, remember?”

Ah.

“Changes that drastically affect one’s existence tend to leave scars. For example... recognizing one’s former employer.”

But despite calling it a scar, the agent’s expression wasn’t harsh.

She even leaned toward the cage and said—

“And I should thank you.”

...Thank me?

“For sending the citizens caught up in the resort incident back home safely.”

......

“You hired the adults even without payment, and gave the kids vouchers for a night’s stay.”

[My word. Friend, are you running a business out of kindness and dirt alone?]

“If that had happened in a Red Zone, it would’ve registered as a supernatural disaster. But thanks to you, it’s been officially logged as a ‘phenomenon.’”

In a strange, confusing rush of emotion, I looked at Team Leader Haegŭm.

Yeah.

That was a choice I made back then.

And I was still the owner of that resort.

Even now.

Even after all that happened.

“So, that’s why...”

......

“No matter how I think about it, the resort owner just doesn’t seem like a good match for Baekilmong Corporation.”

Ah.

“Are you bound by contract?”

...!
